\section{Physics-Inspired Neural Networks}

Wir haben in Abschnitt \ref{chapter:Roboterherleitung} gesehen, dass die Dynmaik
eines einfachen Roboters über eine Euler-Lagrange-Gleichung definiert wurde. 
Um eine solche Dynamik effizient zu lernen und gleichzeitig zu grantieren, dass diese
physikalisch sinnvoll ist konstruieren wir die Struktur direkt in der Architektur des 
neuronalen Netzes. 
Die Euler-Lagrange Gleichung als ODE 2. Ordnung lautet (\ref{eq:ELE_2nd_Order}):
%
\begin{align}
    \frac{\partial L(\bm{q},\bm{\dot{q}})}{\partial \bm{\dot{q}}^2}
    \cdot 
    \bm{\ddot{q}}
    +
    \frac{\partial L(\bm{q},\bm{\dot{q}})}{\partial \bm{q} \partial \bm{\dot{q}}}
    \cdot 
    \bm{\dot{q}}
    -
    \frac{\partial L(\bm{q},\bm{\dot{q}})}{\partial \bm{q}}
    =
    \bm{u}(t).
\end{align}

Für die spezielle Lagrangefunktion aus Satz \ref{satz:ELE_2nd_Order} lässt sich 
die Gleichung schreiben als:
%
\begin{align*}
    L_(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}})
    =
    T(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}})
    -
    V(\bm{q})
    =
    \frac{1}{2} \bm{\dot{q}}^T M(\bm{q}) \bm{\dot{q}} 
    -
    V(\bm{q})
\end{align*}

Die entsprechende Gleichung lautet dann 

\subsection{Parametrisierung des Neural Networks}

Wir diskutieren nun zwei verschiedene Ansätze zur Parametrisierung der Lagrangefunktion.
Im ersten Fall eine vollständige Parametrisierung von $L$ durch ein MLP (siehe \cite{LLN}) in Gleichung
(\ref{eq:ELE_2nd_Order}) und anschließend 
die Parametrisierung von durch $T$ und $V$ direkt (siehe \cite{Lutter}) in 
Gleichung (\ref{eq:ELE_2nd_Order_structured}).
Wir definieren dazu:
% q,q' = x
\begin{align}
    \bm{q},\bm{\dot{q}}:[t_0, t_f] \to \mathbb{R}^n, 
    \bm{x}:[t_0, t_f] \to \mathbb{R}^{2n}
    \quad
    \bm{x}(t)
    :=
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{q}(t) \\
        \bm{\dot{q}}(t)
    \end{bmatrix}
    \label{eq:def_x}
\end{align}

\subsubsection{Arbitrary Lagrangian Network (A-LNN)}

Wir nutzen zur Parametrisierung von $L:\mathbb{R}^{2n} \to \mathbb{R}$ 
ein MLP mit einem Hidden-Layer (\ref{eq:MLP_One_hidden_layer}):
%
\begin{align*}
    L_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}}) 
    = 
    W^{(N)} 
    \bm{\sigma}
    \left(
        W^{(1)}     
        \begin{bmatrix}
            \bm{q} \\
            \bm{\dot{q}}
        \end{bmatrix} 
        + b^{(1)} 
    \right)
    + b^{(N)}
    %\\
    %W^{(1)} &\in \mathbb{R}^{h \times 2n} , W^{(N)} \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times h},
    %b^{(1)} \in \mathbb{R}^{h}, b^{(N)} \in \mathbb{R}^{1} 
    %\\
    %\bm{\theta} 
    %&=
    %\begin{pmatrix}
    %    W^{(1)} , b^{(1)} , W^{(N)} , b^{(N)}
    %\end{pmatrix}
    %\in \mathbb{R}^{2hn +2h +1}
\end{align*}

Wir definieren die Hilfsgrößen:
% C, M, G
\begin{align*}
    C_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}}) 
    :=
    \nabla_{\bm{\dot{q}}} \nabla_{\bm{q}}^T L_{\bm{\theta}}
    \quad
    M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q},\bm{\dot{q}})
    :=
    \nabla_{\bm{\dot{q}}} \nabla_{\bm{\dot{q}}}^T L_{\bm{\theta}}
    \quad
    G_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}}) 
    :=
    \nabla_{\bm{q}} L_{\bm{\theta}}
\end{align*}

Setzen wir dies in die Euler-Lagrange Gleichung (\ref{eq:ELE}) ein, so folgt:
%
\begin{align*}
    C(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}})
    \cdot \bm{\dot{q}}
    +
    M(\bm{q},\bm{\dot{q}})
    \cdot \bm{\ddot{q}}
    = 
    G_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}})  + \bm{u}
    \Rightarrow
    \bm{\ddot{q}} 
    =
    M^{-1}(\bm{q},\bm{\dot{q}})
    \cdot
    \left(
        G_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}})  - C(\bm{q},\bm{\dot{q}}) \cdot \bm{\dot{q}} + \bm{u}
    \right)
\end{align*}

Dies entspricht der Formulierung der Neural ODE aus (\ref{eq:Neural_Dynamic}).
% dq''/dt = 1/H (L_q - Cq' +u)
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\mathsf{d} \bm{x}(t)}{\dt}
    =
    \bm{f}_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}, \bm{u})
    =
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{x}_2 \\
        M_{\bm{\theta}}^{-1}
        \cdot
        \left(
            G_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}) - C_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}) \bm{x}_2 + \bm{u}
        \right)
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

Gegeben seien $M$ Trajektorien mit $N$ Datenpunkten, dann definieren wir
Loss Funktion über den Mean-Squared-Error, wobei wir im Fall des Trajektorien-Loss
erneut (\ref{Gradientherleitung_Optimal_Control}) verwenden:

% Die 2 verschiedenen Loss Ansätze Regression vs Trajektorienloss
\begin{center}
    \begin{tabular}{l l l}
        & 
        Regression 
        & 
        Trajektorien 
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        Loss  
        & 
        $\begin{aligned}
            \sum_{j=0}^{M-1} 
            \sum_{i=0}^{N-1}
            \norm{
                \bm{\ddot{q}}^{(j)}_{i,\bm{\theta}} - \bm{\ddot{q}}^{(j)}_{i, \text{data}}
                }^2
        \end{aligned}$ 
        & 
        $\begin{aligned}
            \sum_{j=0}^{M-1} \int_{t_0}^{t_f} \norm{\bm{q}_{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}(t) - \bm{q}_{data}^{(j)}(t)}^2 \dt
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        Gradient
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{1}{M}
            \sum_{j=0}^{M-1} \sum_{i=0}^{N-1} 
            \left(\bm{p}_i^{(j)}\right)^T
            \cdot
            \left[
                - \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
                \bm{\ddot{q}_{\theta}}
                +
                \frac{\partial N_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
            \right]
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{1}{M}
            \sum_{j=0}^{M-1}
            \int_{t_0}^{t_f} 
            \left[
                \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}^{(j)}(t)) 
            \right]^T 
            \bm{p}^{(j)}(t) \dt
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        Adjoint
        &
        $
        \begin{aligned}
            \bm{p} := 2H^{-1}_{\bm{\theta}} 
            \left(
                \bm{\ddot{q}} - \bm{\ddot{q}}_{(\text{data})}
            \right)
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $
        \begin{aligned}
            \bm{\dot{p}}(t)
            &= 
            -2 
            \begin{bmatrix}
                \bm{q}_{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}(t) - \bm{q}_{data}^{(j)}(t) \\
                \bm{0}_{n \times 1}
            \end{bmatrix}
            - \left(\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial \bm{x}}\right)^T \bm{p}(t)
            \\
            \bm{p}(t_f) &= \bm{0}_{2n \times 1}
        \end{aligned}
        $
    \end{tabular}
\end{center}
%

Für die Berechnung des Gradienten: 
% Kettenregel für Gradienten dJ/dtheta:
\begin{align*}
    \nabla_{\bm{\theta}} J(\bm{\theta}) 
    &=
    \sum_{j=0}^{M-1} \sum_{i=0}^{N-1} 
    2 \cdot
    \left[
        \bm{\ddot{q}}_{\bm{\theta}}
        (\bm{q}_i^{(j)},\bm{\dot{q}}_i^{(j)},\bm{u}_i^{(j)}) 
        - 
        \bm{\ddot{q}}^{(j)}_{i}
    \right]^T
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial}{\partial \bm{\theta}} \bm{\ddot{q}}_{\bm{\theta}}
    (\bm{q}_i^{(j)},\bm{\dot{q}}_i^{(j)},\bm{u}_i^{(j)})
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitung nach den Parametern gilt nach der Produktregel:
% dq''/dtheta:
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{\ddot{q}}_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}} 
    &=
    \frac{\partial}{\partial \bm{\theta}} 
    \left(
        M_{\bm{\theta}}^{-1}
        \cdot
        \left[
            \underbrace{
                G_{\bm{\theta}} 
                - C_{\bm{\theta}} \bm{\dot{q}} 
                + \bm{u}
            }_{
                := N_{\bm{\theta}}
            }
        \right]
    \right)
    \\
    &=
    \frac{\partial}{\partial \bm{\theta}} \left(M_{\bm{\theta}}^{-1} \right)
    \cdot 
    N_{\bm{\theta}}
    + 
    M_{\bm{\theta}}^{-1}
    \cdot
    \frac{\partial}{\partial \bm{\theta}} N_{\bm{\theta}}
    \\
    &=
    - M_{\bm{\theta}}^{-1} 
    \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
    \underbrace{
        M_{\bm{\theta}}^{-1} 
        N_{\bm{\theta}}
    }_{
        \bm{\ddot{q}_{\theta}}
    }
    +
    M_{\bm{\theta}}^{-1}
    \frac{\partial N_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}} 
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{\ddot{q}}_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
    &=
    M_{\bm{\theta}}^{-1}
    \cdot
    \left[
        - \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
        \bm{\ddot{q}_{\theta}}
        +
        \frac{\partial N_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
    \right]
\end{align*}

Da $M$ als Hesse-Matrix symmetrisch ist, gilt $M^{-1} = M^{-T}$. Damit: 
% dJ/dtheta:
\begin{align*}
    \nabla_{\bm{\theta}} J(\bm{\theta})
    &=
    \sum_{j=0}^{M-1} \sum_{i=0}^{N-1} 
    \underbrace{
        2 \cdot
        \left[
            \bm{\ddot{q}}_{\bm{\theta}}
            (\bm{q}_i^{(j)},\bm{\dot{q}}_i^{(j)},\bm{u}_i^{(j)}) 
            - 
            \bm{\ddot{q}}^{(j)}_{i}
        \right]^T
    H_{\bm{\theta}}^{-1}
    }_{\left(\bm{p}_i^{(j)}\right)^T}
    \cdot
    \left[
        - \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
        \bm{\ddot{q}_{\theta}}
        +
        \frac{\partial N_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
    \right]
    \\
    \nabla_{\bm{\theta}} J(\bm{\theta})
    &=
    \sum_{j=0}^{M-1} \sum_{i=0}^{N-1} \left(\bm{p}_i^{(j)}\right)^T 
    \cdot
    \left[
        - \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
        \bm{\ddot{q}_{\theta}}
        +
        \frac{\partial N_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
    \right]
    \quad
    \bm{p} := 2M^{-1}_{\bm{\theta}} 
    \left(
        \bm{\ddot{q}} - \bm{\ddot{q}}_{(\text{data})}
    \right)
\end{align*}

% Berechnung Gradient Trajektorien Loss 

\subsubsection{Deep Lagrangian Networks (DeLaN)}

Wir nutzen die Form der Lagrange-Funktion aus (\ref{eq:Lagrangian}):
%
\begin{align*}
    L_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}})
    =
    T_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}, \bm{\dot{q}})
    -
    V_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q})
    =
    \frac{1}{2} \bm{\dot{q}}^T M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}) \bm{\dot{q}} 
    -
    V_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q})
\end{align*}

um zu garantieren, dass $M$ symmetrisch und positiv definit ist wählen wir $M_{\bm{\theta}}$
als
%
\begin{align}\label{def:NN_M}
    M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q})
    =
    A_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}) A_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q})^T
    + \epsilon \bm{I}_n
    \quad
    a_{\bm{\theta},ij}
    =
    W^{(N, ij)} \cdot \bm{\sigma}\left(W^{(1,ij)} \bm{q} + b^{(1,ij)}\right) + b^{(N,ij)}
\end{align}

wobei $A_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q})$ eine untere Dreiecksmatrix ist.\footnote{
    A matrix $L \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times n}$ is called a lower triangular matrix, 
    if $L_{ij} = 0$ for all $i < j$.
    %
} 
Weiter gelte für die Diagonalelemente:
%
\begin{align*}
    (A_{\bm{\theta}})_{ii} = \mathsf{softplus}\left(a_{\bm{\theta},ii} + \alpha \right) + \epsilon
\end{align*}

Für die potenzielle Energie wählen wir ein beliebiges MLP: 
%
\begin{align}\label{def:NN_V}
    V_{\bm{\theta}}
    =
    W^{(N)}_V \cdot \bm{\sigma}\left(W^{(1)}_V \bm{q} + b^{(1)}_V\right) + b^{(N)}_V
\end{align}

Die Euler-Lagrange-Gleichung liefert:
%
\begin{align*}
    M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{q}) \bm{\ddot{q}} 
\end{align*}


\subsection{Herleitung im eindimensionalen Fall}

Sei $\bm{x}(t) = \begin{bmatrix} q(t) & \dot{q}(t) \end{bmatrix}^T \in \mathbb{R}^2$ gegeben. 
Für die Lagrangefunktion ergibt sich die folgende Struktur:
% Definition von L als MLP
\begin{align*}
    L(\bm{x})
    =
    L_{\bm{\theta}}(q(t), \dot{q}) 
    &= 
    W^{(N)} 
    \bm{\sigma}
    \left(
        W^{(1)} 
        \begin{bmatrix} 
            q(t) \\
            \dot{q}(t)
        \end{bmatrix}
        + 
        b^{(1)}
    \right)
    + b^{(N)}
    \\
    W^{(1)} \in & \mathbb{R}^{h \times 2}, b^{(1)} \in \mathbb{R}^{h}, W^{(N)} \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times h},
    b^{(N)} \in \mathbb{R}
\end{align*}


Bezeichne $\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(s)}$ Spalte $s$ der Matrix $W^{(1)}$.
Für die ersten Ableitungen der Lagrange-Funktion nach dem Zustand gilt:
% dLdq und dLdq' 
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial L(\bm{x})}{\partial q}
    &=
    \sum_{k=1}^{h}
    W^{(N)}_{1k} 
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial \sigma}{\partial z} \left(\bm{z}_k\right)
    \cdot 
    W^{(1)}_{k1} 
    =
    W^{(N)} \cdot 
    \left(
        \bm{\alpha} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}
    \right)
    \\
    \frac{\partial L(\bm{x})}{\partial \dot{q}}
    &=
    \sum_{k=1}^{h}
    W^{(N)}_{1k}
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial \sigma}{\partial z} (\bm{z}_k)
    \cdot 
    W^{(1)}_{k2}
    =
    W^{(N)} \cdot 
    \left(
        \bm{\alpha} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
    \right)
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitung nach $\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}$ gilt komponentenweise und in Vektorform:
% Exemplarische komponentenweise Berechnung der Ableitung W^1_k1 von dL/dq
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial}{\partial W_{k1}^{(1)}} \frac{\partial L(\bm{x})}{\partial q}
    &=
    W^{(N)}_{1k} 
    \cdot
    \left[
        \frac{\partial \bm{\alpha}_k (z)}{\partial z} \bigg|_{z = \bm{z}_k} 
        \cdot
        \frac{\partial \bm{z}_k}{\partial W_{k1}^{(1)}} 
        \cdot 
        W_{k1}^{(1)}
        +
        \bm{\alpha}_k 
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial W_{k1}^{(1)}}{\partial W_{k1}^{(1)}}
    \right]
    =
    W^{(N)}_{1k} 
    \cdot
    \left[
        \bm{\beta}_k
        \cdot
        \bm{x}_1
        \cdot 
        W_{k1}^{(1)}
        +
        \bm{\alpha}_k 
    \right]
    \\
    \frac{\partial}{\partial \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}} \frac{\partial L(\bm{x})}{\partial q}
    &=
    \begin{bmatrix}
        W_{11}^{(N)} \\
        \vdots \\
        W_{1h}^{(N)}
    \end{bmatrix}
    \odot
    \left[
        \bm{\beta} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{x}_1 + \bm{\alpha}
    \right]
    =
    \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T 
    \odot
    \left[
        \bm{\beta} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{x}_1 + \bm{\alpha}
    \right]
\end{align*}

Zusammenfassen der Ableitungen nach den Parametern $\bm{\theta}$:

% Tabelle mit den Ableitungen d/dtheta dL/dq
\begin{center}
    \begin{tabular}{l| l }
        $\bm{\theta}$ 
        & 
        $\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial}{\partial \theta} \frac{\partial L}{\partial q} \end{aligned}$ 
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned} \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \end{aligned}$ 
        & 
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot
            \left[
                \bm{\beta} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{x}_1 + \bm{\alpha}
            \right]
        \end{aligned}$ 
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned} \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \end{aligned}$ 
        & 
        $\begin{aligned} 
            \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot
            \left[
                \bm{\beta} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{x}_2
            \right]
        \end{aligned}$ 
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned} b^{(1)} \end{aligned}$ 
        & 
        $\begin{aligned} 
            \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot
            \left[
                \bm{\beta} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} 
            \right]
        \end{aligned}$ 
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned} W^{(N)} \end{aligned}$ 
        & 
        $\begin{aligned}
            \bm{\alpha} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}
        \end{aligned}$ 
    \end{tabular}
\end{center}


Für die zweiten Ableitungen der Lagrange-Funktion nach dem Zustand gilt:
% C = d^2L/dq'dq und H = d^2L/dq'^2
\begin{align*}
    C
    &=
    \frac{\partial^2 L(\bm{x})}{\partial \dot{q} \partial q}
    =
    \sum_{k=1}^{h}
    W^{(N)}_{1k} 
    \cdot
    \frac{\partial \bm{\alpha}_k}{\partial z}(\bm{z}_k)
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial \bm{z}_k}{\partial \bm{x}_2}
    \cdot
    W^{(1)}_{k1}
    = 
    W^{(N)} 
    \cdot 
    \left(
        \bm{\beta}
        \odot
        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}
        \odot 
        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
    \right)
    \\
    H
    &=
    \frac{\partial^2 L(\bm{x})}{\partial \dot{q}^2}
    =
    \sum_{k=1}^{h} W_{1k}^{(N)} 
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial \bm{\alpha}_k}{\partial z}(\bm{z}_k)
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial \bm{z}_k}{\partial \bm{x}_2}
    \cdot
    W^{(1)}_{k2}
    = 
    W^{(N)} \cdot 
    \left(
        \bm{\beta}
        \odot
        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
        \odot 
        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
    \right)
\end{align*}

Es gilt für die Ableitung nach $W^{(1)}$:
% Exemplarische komponentenweise Berechnung der Ableitung W^1_k1
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial C}{\partial W^{(1)}_{k1}}
    &=
    W^{(N)}_{1k} 
    \cdot
    W^{(1)}_{k2}
    \cdot 
    \left[
        \frac{\partial \bm{\beta}_k}{\partial z}(\bm{z}_k)
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial \bm{z}_k}{\partial W^{(1)}_{k1}}
        \cdot 
        W^{(1)}_{k1}
        +
        \bm{\beta}_k 
    \right]
    =
    W^{(N)}_{1k} 
    \cdot
    W^{(1)}_{k2}
    \cdot 
    \left[
        \bm{\gamma}_k
        \cdot 
        \bm{x}_1
        \cdot 
        W^{(1)}_{k1}
        +
        \bm{\beta}_k 
    \right]
    \\
    \frac{\partial H}{\partial W^{(1)}_{k1}}
    &=
    W^{(N)}_{1k} 
    \cdot
    \left(W^{(1)}_{k2}\right)^2
    \cdot 
    \left[
        \frac{\partial \bm{\beta}_k}{\partial z}(\bm{z}_k)
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial \bm{z}_k}{\partial W^{(1)}_{k1}}
    \right]
    =
    W^{(N)}_{1k} 
    \cdot
    \left(W^{(1)}_{k2}\right)^2
    \cdot 
    \left[
        \bm{\gamma}_k
        \cdot 
        \bm{x}_1
    \right]
\end{align*}

Wir fassen die Ableitungen zusammen:

% dC/dtheta und dH/dtheta
\begin{center}
    \footnotesize
    \begin{tabular}{l l l}
        $\bm{\theta}$ 
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial C}{\partial \theta}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial H}{\partial \theta}
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        % dC/dW1^1
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot 
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
            \odot
            \left(
                \bm{\gamma} 
                \odot
                \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}
                \bm{x}_1
                + 
                \bm{\beta}
            \right)
        \end{aligned}$
        & 
        % dH/dW1^1
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot 
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
            \odot 
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
            \odot
            \bm{\gamma}
            \bm{x}_1
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot 
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}
            \odot
            \left(
                \bm{\gamma} 
                \odot
                \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
                \bm{x}_2
                + 
                \bm{\beta}
            \right)
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot 
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
            \odot
            \left(
                \bm{\gamma} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \bm{x}_2
                +
                2 \bm{\beta}
            \right)
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            b^{(1)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T \odot \bm{\gamma} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T \odot \bm{\gamma} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(N)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \bm{\beta} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \bm{\beta} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
        \end{aligned}$
    \end{tabular}
\end{center}

Wir verwenden als Aktivierungsfunktion $\mathsf{softplus}$ und erhalten folgenen Algorithmus:

% Algorithmus - Regression
\begin{algorithm}\label{alg:LNN_Regression}
    \footnotesize
    \caption{LNN with pointwise regression loss}
    \begin{algorithmic}[1]
        \For {epoch in Episodes}
            \State \textbf{Forward Pass}: Given $M$ trajectories with $N$ time points, 
            $\bm{X} = \begin{bmatrix} \bm{q} & \bm{\dot{q}} \end{bmatrix}^T \in \mathbb{R}^{2 \times K}$
            %
            \State \indent 
            $\bm{Z} = W^{(1)} \cdot \bm{X} + b^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_K^T$
            \State \indent
            $\begin{aligned}
                \bm{A} &= \log\left(\bm{1}_{h \times K} + \exp\left(\bm{Z}\right)\right) 
            \end{aligned}$
            \State \indent
            $\begin{aligned}
                \bm{\alpha} &= \frac{\exp\left(\bm{Z}\right)}{\bm{1}_{h \times K} + \exp\left(\bm{Z}\right)} 
            \end{aligned}$
            \State \indent
            $\begin{aligned}
                \bm{\beta} &= \bm{\alpha} \odot (\bm{1}_{h \times K} - \bm{\alpha}) 
            \end{aligned}$
            \State \indent
            $\begin{aligned}
                \bm{\gamma} &= \bm{\beta} \odot (\bm{1}_{h \times K} - \bm{2}_{h \times K} \odot \bm{\alpha})
            \end{aligned}$
            \State \textbf{Compute derivatives of L:}
            \State \indent
            $\begin{aligned}
                \partial_{q} \bm{L} 
                =
                W^{(N)} \left[\bm{\alpha} \odot \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_K^T\right) \right]
            \end{aligned}$
            \State \indent
            $\begin{aligned}
                \bm{C}
                =
                W^{(N)}
                \cdot 
                \left[
                    \bm{\beta}
                    \odot
                    \left(        
                        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_K^T
                    \right)
                    \odot 
                    \left(
                        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_K^T
                    \right)
                \right]
            \end{aligned}$
            \State \indent
            $\begin{aligned}
                \bm{H}
                =
                W^{(N)}
                \cdot 
                \left[
                    \bm{\beta}
                    \odot
                    \left(        
                        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
                        \cdot
                        \bm{1}_K^T
                    \right)
                    \odot 
                    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_K^T\right)
                \right]
            \end{aligned}$
            \State Compute the prediction 
            $\begin{aligned}
                \bm{\ddot{q}} = \frac{\partial_{\bm{q}}\bm{L} - \bm{C} \bm{\dot{q}} + \bm{u}}{\bm{H}}
            \end{aligned}$
            \Comment{Possible because H is a scalar component wise}
            \State \textbf{Gradient Computation}: 
            $\begin{aligned}
                \frac{1}{K} \sum_{k=1}^{K} 2 \cdot \frac{\bm{\ddot{q}} - \bm{\ddot{q}}_{\text{data}}}{\bm{H}}
                \cdot 
                \left[
                    -
                    \frac{\partial \bm{H}}{\partial \bm{\theta}} \cdot \bm{\ddot{q}} 
                    + 
                    \frac{\partial}{\partial \bm{\theta}} \partial_{q} \bm{L} 
                    -
                    \frac{\partial \bm{C}}{\partial \bm{\theta}} \cdot \bm{\dot{q}} 
                \right]
            \end{aligned}$
            \State Update the Parameter $\bm{\theta}$ using gradient descent
        \EndFor
        \State \textbf{Output}: Trained Parameters $\bm{\theta} = (W^{(1)}, b^{(1)}, W^{(N)}, b^{(N)})$
    \end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}


% Ab hier nur für den Adjoint Approach wichtig:

Für die Ableitung der Funktion $\bm{f}$ gilt: 
% Berechnung f_x
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial \bm{x}}
    &=
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \partial_{q} \dot{q}
        &
        \partial_{\dot{q}} \dot{q}
        \\
        \partial_{q} \ddot{q}
        &
        \partial_{\dot{q}} \ddot{q}
    \end{bmatrix}
    \\
    &=
    \begin{bmatrix}
        0
        &
        1
        \\
        \frac{\partial}{\partial q}  H^{-1} \left(\frac{\partial L}{\partial q} - C \dot{q} + u\right)
        &
        \frac{\partial}{\partial \dot{q}}  H^{-1} \left(\frac{\partial L}{\partial q} - C \dot{q} + u\right)
    \end{bmatrix}
    \\
    &=
    \begin{bmatrix}
        0
        &
        1
        \\
        \frac{-1}{H^2} \frac{\partial H}{\partial q} 
        \left(
            \frac{\partial L}{\partial q} - C \dot{q} + u
        \right)
        + 
        \frac{1}{H}
        \left(
            \frac{\partial^2 L}{\partial q^2} - \frac{\partial C}{\partial q} \dot{q}
        \right)
        &
        \frac{-1}{H^2} \frac{\partial H}{\partial \dot{q}} 
        \left(
            \frac{\partial L}{\partial q} - C \dot{q} + u
        \right)
        + 
        \frac{1}{H}
        \left(
            C - \frac{\partial C}{\partial \dot{q}} \dot{q}
            - C
        \right)
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

Wir verwenden erneut die Kettenregel mit $\frac{\partial \bm{z}}{\partial q} = \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}$ und 
$\frac{\partial \bm{z}}{\partial \dot{q}} = \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}$

\begin{center}
    \begin{tabular}{l l}
        Ausdruck 
        &
        Vektorform
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial H}{\partial q}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(N)} \left(\bm{\gamma} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}\right)
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial^2 L}{\partial q^2}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(N)} \left(\bm{\beta} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}\right)
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial C}{\partial q}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(N)} \left(\bm{\gamma} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}\right)
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial H}{\partial \dot{q}}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(N)} \left(\bm{\gamma} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}\right)
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial C}{\partial \dot{q}}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(N)} \left(\bm{\gamma} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}\right)
        \end{aligned}$
    \end{tabular}
\end{center}

Es gilt für die Kontraktion $\bm{f}_{\bm{x}}^T \bm{p}$:
% f_x * p
\begin{align*}
    \bm{f}_{\bm{x}}^T \cdot
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{p}_1 \\
        \bm{p}_2
    \end{bmatrix}
    =
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{p}_2
        \cdot
        \left(
            -\frac{1}{H^2} \frac{\partial H}{\partial q} 
            \left(
                \frac{\partial L}{\partial q} - C \dot{q} + u
            \right)
            + 
            \frac{1}{H}
            \left(
                \frac{\partial^2 L}{\partial q^2} - \frac{\partial C}{\partial q} \dot{q}
            \right)
        \right)
        \\
        \bm{p}_1 
        +
        \bm{p}_2
        \cdot
        \left(
            -\frac{1}{H^2} \frac{\partial H}{\partial \dot{q}} 
            \left(
                \frac{\partial L}{\partial q} - C \dot{q} + u
            \right)
            +
            \frac{1}{H}
            \left(
                C - \frac{\partial C}{\partial \dot{q}} \dot{q}
                - C
            \right)
        \right)
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

Für die adjungierte Gleichung gilt:
% p' = -L_x - f_x^T p
\begin{align*}
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{\dot{p}}_1 \\
        \bm{\dot{p}}_2
    \end{bmatrix}
    =
    -2 \cdot
    \begin{bmatrix}
        q_{\bm{\theta}}(t) - q_{\text{data}}(t) \\
        0
    \end{bmatrix}
    - 
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{p}_2
        \cdot
        \left(
            -\frac{1}{H^2} \frac{\partial H}{\partial q} 
            \left(
                \frac{\partial L}{\partial q} - C \dot{q} + u
            \right)
            + 
            \frac{1}{H}
            \left(
                \frac{\partial^2 L}{\partial q^2} - \frac{\partial C}{\partial q} \dot{q}
            \right)
        \right)
        \\
        \bm{p}_1 
        +
        \bm{p}_2
        \cdot
        \left(
            -\frac{1}{H^2} \frac{\partial H}{\partial \dot{q}} 
            \left(
                \frac{\partial L}{\partial q} - C \dot{q} + u
            \right)
            +
            \frac{1}{H}
            \left(
                C - \frac{\partial C}{\partial \dot{q}} \dot{q}
                - C
            \right)
        \right)
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

Es gilt für die Kontraktion $\bm{f}_{\bm{\theta}}^T \bm{p}$:
% f_theta^T * p
\begin{align*}
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{0}_{1 \times (4h+1)}
        \\
        \left(\partial_{\bm{\theta}} \ddot{q}\right)
    \end{bmatrix}^T
    \cdot
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{p}_1 \\
        \bm{p}_2
    \end{bmatrix}
    =
    \left(\partial_{\bm{\theta}} \ddot{q}\right)^T \cdot \bm{p}_2(t)
    =
    \frac{1}{H} \cdot
    \left[
        - 
        \frac{\partial H}{\partial \bm{\theta}} \ddot{q} 
        + 
        \frac{\partial}{\partial \bm{\theta}} 
        \left(\partial_q L - C \dot{q} + u \right)
    \right]
    \bm{p}_2
\end{align*}

Wir verwenden als Aktivierungsfunktion erneut $\mathsf{softplus}$.
%\newpage

% Algorithmus - Adjoint Verfahren
\begin{algorithm*}\label{alg:LNN_adjoint}
    \footnotesize
    \caption{LNN with trajectory based adjoint approach}
    \begin{algorithmic}[1]
        \State \textbf{Dataset}: $M$ Trajectories with $N$ time steps. 
        \State Set $\bm{X}_0 \in \mathbb{R}^{2 \times M}$ initial values of the trajectories
        % Hier gehts ins Gradientenverfahren:
        \For {epoch in Episodes}
            \State \textbf{Forward Pass}: Save $\bm{X} \in \mathbb{R}^{N\times 2 \times M}$
            \For {$i$ from $0$ to $N-2$}
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{Z}_i = W^{(1)} \bm{X}_i + b^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T
                \end{aligned}$
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{A}_i = \log\left(\bm{1}_{h \times M} + \exp\left(\bm{Z}_i\right)\right)
                \end{aligned}$
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{\alpha}_i = \frac{\exp\left(\bm{Z}_i\right)}{\bm{1}_{h \times M} + \exp\left(\bm{Z}_i\right)}
                \end{aligned}$
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{\beta}_i = \bm{\alpha}_i \cdot \left(\bm{1}_{h \times M} - \bm{\alpha}_i \right)
                \end{aligned}$
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{\gamma}_i = \bm{\beta}_i \cdot \left(\bm{1}_{h \times M} - 2 \cdot \bm{\alpha}_i \right)
                \end{aligned}$
                \State Compute $\bm{H}_i, \bm{C}_i, \partial_q \bm{L}_i$ based on $\bm{\alpha}_i, \bm{\beta}_i, \bm{\gamma}_i$
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{X}_{i+1} 
                    =
                    \begin{bmatrix}
                        \bm{Q}_{i+1} \\
                        \bm{\dot{Q}}_{i+1}
                    \end{bmatrix}
                    =
                    \begin{bmatrix}
                        \bm{Q}_{i} \\
                        \bm{\dot{Q}}_{i}
                    \end{bmatrix}
                    + \Delta t
                    \begin{bmatrix}
                        \bm{\dot{Q}}_{i} \\
                        \frac{1}{\bm{H}_i} \left(\partial_q \bm{L}_i - \bm{C}_i \bm{\dot{Q}}_i + \bm{U}_i \right) 
                    \end{bmatrix}
                \end{aligned}$
                \State Compute $\bm{\ddot{Q}}_i = \frac{\partial_q \bm{L}_i - \bm{C}_i \bm{\dot{Q}}_i + \bm{U}_i}{\bm{H}_i}$
            \EndFor
 
            \State \textbf{Backward Pass}: Save $\bm{P} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times 2 \times M}$
            \For {$i$ from $N-1$ to $1$}
                \State
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \begin{bmatrix}
                            \bm{P}_{1,i-1} \\
                            \bm{P}_{2,i-1}
                    \end{bmatrix}
                    =
                    \begin{bmatrix}
                        \bm{P}_{1,i} \\
                        \bm{P}_{2,i}
                    \end{bmatrix}
                    + \Delta t \cdot 
                    \left(
                        2
                        \cdot 
                        \begin{bmatrix}
                            \bm{Q}_{i,\bm{\theta}} - \bm{Q}_{i,\text{data}} \\
                            \bm{0}_{1 \times M}
                        \end{bmatrix}
                        +
                        \bm{f}_{\bm{x}}\left(\bm{X}_i\right)^T \bm{P}_i
                    \right)
                    \quad
                    \bm{P}_{N-1}
                    =
                    \bm{0}_{2 \times M}
                \end{aligned}$
            \EndFor
            \State Perform gradient descent with 
            $\begin{aligned}
                \nabla_{\bm{\theta}} J 
                = 
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-1} \Delta t_i \cdot 
                \frac{1}{\bm{H}_i} \cdot
                \left[
                    - 
                    \frac{\partial \bm{H}_i}{\partial \bm{\theta}} \bm{\ddot{Q}}_i
                    + 
                    \frac{\partial}{\partial \bm{\theta}} 
                    \left(\partial_q \bm{L}_i - \bm{C}_i \bm{\dot{Q}}_i + \bm{U}_i \right)
                \right]
                \bm{P}_{2,i}^T
            \end{aligned}$
        \EndFor
        \State \textbf{Output}: Trained Parameters $\bm{\theta} = (W^{(1)}, b^{(1)}, W^{(N)}, b^{(N)})$
    \end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm*}

\subsection{Strukturierte Lagrange Funktion}

Wir verwenden den Ansatz von \cite{Lutter} für einen strukturierten Ansatz 
für die Lagrange-Funktion:
%
\begin{align*}
    L_{\bm{\theta}}(q,\dot{q})
    &=
    \frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}(q) \dot{q}^2 - V_{\bm{\theta}}(q)
    \quad
    M_{\bm{\theta}}(q) 
    = 
    A(q)^2 
    +
    \epsilon
    =
    \left(
        \log\left(1 + e^{z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)} \right)
    \right)^2
    + \epsilon,
    \quad
    \epsilon > 0 
\end{align*}

mit der Parametrisierung:
% z, V als MLP
\begin{align*}
    z_{\bm{\theta}} 
    &= W^{(N)}_z \cdot \bm{\sigma}\left(W^{(1)}_z q + b_z^{(1)}\right) + b_z^{(N)}
    \\
    V_{\bm{\theta}} 
    &= W^{(N)}_V \cdot \bm{\sigma}\left(W^{(1)}_V q + b_V^{(1)}\right) + b_V^{(N)}
    \\
    W^{(N)}_z ,& W^{(N)}_V \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times h}, W^{(1)}_z , 
    W^{(1)}_V \in \mathbb{R}^{h \times 1} ,
    b_z^{(1)} , b_V^{(1)} \in \mathbb{R}^{h} 
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitung von $M_{\bm{\theta}}$ nach $q$ gilt:
% M'
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial q}
    &=
    2A(q) A'(q)
    =
    2A(q) \cdot s(q) \cdot 
    \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q} 
    \quad
    s(q)
    =
    \frac{e^{z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}}{1 + e^{z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}}
\end{align*}

Für die zweite Ableitung nach $q$ gilt:\footnote{
    Denote $A'(q) = s(q) \cdot z_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)$. Using the differentiation 
    rules for the softplus function leads to
    %
    \begin{align*}
        s'(q) 
        =
        \frac{\partial s(q)}{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}} 
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q}
        =
        s(q) \cdot (1-s(q)) \cdot \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q} 
    \end{align*}
    %
    Using the product rule to compute the 2nd derivative of $A(q)$
    %
    \begin{align*}
        A''(q) 
        =
        s'(q) \cdot z_{\bm{\theta}}'(q) + s(q) \cdot z_{\bm{\theta}}''(q)
        =
        s(q) \cdot (1-s(q)) \cdot 
        \left(
            \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q} 
        \right)^2
        +
        s(q) 
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial^2 z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q^2}
    \end{align*}
}
% Ableitung M''
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial^2 M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial q^2}
    &=
    2 \cdot 
    \left[
        s(q)^2 
        \cdot 
        \left(
            \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q} 
        \right)^2
        + 
        A(q) \cdot s(q) \cdot (1-s(q)) \cdot 
        \left(
            \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q} 
        \right)^2
        +
        A(q) \cdot s(q) 
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial^2 z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q^2}
    \right]
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitungen eines Netzes nach dem Zustand gilt:
% dz/dq oder dV/dq
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q}
    &=
    \sum_{k=1}^{h}
    W_{z,1k}^{(N)} 
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial \bm{\sigma}_k (\bm{z})}{\partial \bm{z}} \bigg|_{\bm{z} = W_z^{(1)}q + b^{(1)}_z}
    \cdot
    \frac{\partial W_{z,k1}^{(1)} \cdot q}{\partial q}
    =
    W_z^{(N)} \cdot \left(\bm{\alpha}_z \odot W_z^{(1)}\right)
    \\
    \frac{\partial^2 z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q^2}
    &=
    \sum_{k=1}^{h}
    W_{z,1k}^{(N)} 
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial \bm{\alpha}_k (\bm{z})}{\partial \bm{z}} \bigg|_{\bm{z} = W_z^{(1)}q + b^{(1)}_z}
    \cdot
    \frac{\partial W_{z,k1}^{(1)} \cdot q}{\partial q}
    =
    W_z^{(N)} \cdot \left(\bm{\beta}_z \odot W_z^{(1)} \odot W_z^{(1)}\right)
\end{align*}


Wir halten die Ableitungen der Netze fest:

% Tabelle mit den Ableitungen:
\begin{center}
    \begin{tabular}{ll}
        Ausdruck 
        &
        Matrizenausdruck
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        % Hier die Zeile von von A ---------------------------------        
        $A(q)$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \log
            \left(
                1 + 
                \exp
                \left(
                    W^{(N)}_z \cdot 
                    \bm{\sigma}
                    \left(
                        W^{(1)}_z q + b_z^{(1)}
                    \right) 
                    + b_z^{(N)}
                \right)
            \right)
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        % Hier die Zeile von von s ---------------------------------        
        $s(q)$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{1}{
                1 
                + 
                \exp
                \left(
                    - W^{(N)}_z \cdot \bm{\sigma}
                    \left(
                        W^{(1)}_z q 
                        +
                        b_z^{(1)}
                    \right) 
                    - b_z^{(N)}
                \right)}
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        % Hier die Zeile von von M ---------------------------------        
        $M_{\bm{\theta}}(q)$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            A(q)^2
            + \epsilon
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        % Hier die Zeile von von z' ---------------------------------
        $z_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            W_z^{(N)}
            \cdot
            \left(\bm{\alpha}_z \odot W_z^{(1)}\right)
        \end{aligned}$ 
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        % Hier die Zeile von von z'' ---------------------------------
        $z_{\bm{\theta}}''(q)$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            W_z^{(N)}
            \cdot
            \left(\bm{\beta}_z \odot W_z^{(1)} \odot W_z^{(1)}\right)
        \end{aligned}$ 
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        % Hier die Zeile von von M' ---------------------------------
        $M_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            2 \cdot
            A(q)
            \cdot 
            s(q)
            \cdot 
            \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial q} 
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        % Hier die Zeile von M'' -------------------------------------
        $M_{\bm{\theta}}''(q)$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            2 \cdot 
            \left[
                s(q)^2 
                \cdot 
                z_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)^2
                + 
                A(q) \cdot s(q) \cdot (1-s(q)) \cdot 
                z_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)^2
                +
                A(q) \cdot s(q)
                \cdot 
                z_{\bm{\theta}}''(q)
            \right]
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        % Hier die Zeile von von V' ---------------------------------
        $V_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            W_V^{(N)}
            \cdot
            \left(\bm{\alpha}_V \odot W_V^{(1)}\right)
        \end{aligned}$ 
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        % Hier die Zeile von von V'' ---------------------------------
        $V_{\bm{\theta}}''(q)$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            W_V^{(N)}
            \cdot
            \left(\bm{\beta}_V \odot W_V^{(1)} \odot W_V^{(1)}\right)
        \end{aligned}$ 
    \end{tabular}
\end{center}

Für die Ableitungen der Lagrange Funktion gilt nun
% Berechnung L_q und L_qdot
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial L}{\partial q} 
    &= 
    \frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}'(q) \dot{q}^2 - V_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)
    \\
    \frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot{q}} 
    &= 
    M_{\bm{\theta}}(q) \dot{q} 
\end{align*}

Wir erhalten für die zweite Ableitungen
% Berechnung von C und H 
\begin{align*}
    C
    &=
    \frac{\partial^2 L}{\partial q \partial \dot{q}}
    =
    M_{\bm{\theta}}'(q) \cdot \dot{q}
    \\
    H
    &=
    \frac{\partial^2 L}{\partial \dot{q}^2}
    =
    M_{\bm{\theta}}(q)
\end{align*}

Sei $\bm{x} = \begin{bmatrix} q & \dot{q} \end{bmatrix}^T$ dann lautet die
zweite Komponente $\bm{\dot{x}}_2=\bm{f}_2 (\bm{x})$ mit
$C = M_{\bm{\theta}}'(q) \cdot \dot{q}$ 
und $H = M_{\bm{\theta}}(q)$:
% x'_2 = f_2 
\begin{align}
    \bm{\dot{x}}_2
    &=
    \frac{1}{H_{\bm{\theta}}} \cdot 
    \left[
        \frac{\partial L_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial q} 
        -
        C_{\bm{\theta}} \cdot \bm{x}_2 + u
    \right]
    \nonumber
    \\
    &=
    \frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)} 
    \cdot 
    \left[
        - \frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1) \bm{x}_2^2 
        - V_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1) + u
    \right]
    \label{eq:ELE_structured_L}
\end{align}


Für die Ableitung nach $\bm{\theta}_V$ gilt:
% df_2/dtheta_V
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial \bm{\theta}_V}
    =
    - \frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)} 
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial V_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_V}
    =
    - \frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)} 
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial}{\partial \bm{\theta}_V}
    \left(
        W_V^{(N)} \cdot \left(\bm{\alpha}_V \odot W_V^{(1)}\right)
    \right)
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitung nach der Komponente $W_{V,k1}^{(1)}$ gilt exemplarisch:
% dV/dW1 
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial V_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial W_{V,k1}^{(1)}}
    &=
    \frac{\partial}{\partial W_{V,k1}^{(1)}}
    \left(
        \sum_{k=1}^{h} 
        W_{V, 1k}^{(N)} 
        \cdot
        \bm{\alpha}\left(W_{V,k1}^{(1)} \bm{x}_1 + b^{(1)} \right)
        \cdot 
        W_{V,k1}^{(1)}
    \right)
    \\
    &=
    W_{V, 1k}^{(N)} 
    \cdot
    \left[
        \bm{\beta}\left(W_{V,k1}^{(1)} \bm{x}_1 + b^{(1)} \right)
        \cdot 
        \bm{x}_1 
        \cdot
        W_{V,k1}^{(1)}
        +
        \bm{\alpha}\left(W_{V,k1}^{(1)} \bm{x}_1 + b^{(1)} \right)
    \right]
    \\
    \Rightarrow
    \frac{\partial V_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial W_{V}^{(1)}}
    &=
    \left(W_V^{(N)}\right)^T 
    \odot
    \left[
        \bm{\beta}_V \odot W_V^{(1)} \bm{x}_1 
        +
        \bm{\alpha}_V
    \right]
\end{align*}

Die anderen Ableitungen folgen analog. 
Wir halten diese in folgener Tabelle fest:

% Tabelle für dV'/dtheta_V
\begin{center}
    \begin{tabular}{l l}
        $\bm{\theta}$ 
        & 
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial V_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_V}
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(1)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W_V^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot
            \left[
                \bm{\beta}_V \odot W_V^{(1)} \bm{x}_1 
                +
                \bm{\alpha}_V
            \right]
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            b^{(1)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W_V^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot
            \left[
                \bm{\beta}_V \odot W_V^{(1)}
            \right]
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(N)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(\bm{\alpha}_V \odot W_V^{(1)}\right)^T
        \end{aligned}$
    \end{tabular}
\end{center}

Analog erhalten wir die Ausdrücke für
$\frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}$. 
%
Für $z_{\bm{\theta}}$ bilden wir die Ableitung nach der Komponente $W_{z,k1}^{(1)}$.
%
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial W^{(1)}_{z,k1}}
    &=
    W_{z,1k}^{(N)} 
    \cdot
    \frac{\partial \bm{\sigma}_k}{\partial \bm{z}} \bigg|_{\bm{z}=W_z^{(1)}q + b^{(1)}_z}
    \cdot
    \frac{\partial W_z^{(1)}q}{\partial W^{(1)}_{z,k1}}
    =
    W_{z,1k}^{(N)}
    \cdot 
    \bm{\alpha}_{z,k} \cdot q
    \\
    \Rightarrow
    \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial W^{(1)}_{z}}
    &=
    \left(W_{z}^{(N)}\right)^T
    \odot 
    \bm{\alpha}_{z} \cdot \bm{x}_1
\end{align*}

Die anderen Ableitungen folgen analog. 
Wir halten die Ableitungen in folgender Tabelle fest:

% Tabelle für dz/dtheta_z und dz'/dtheta_z
\begin{center}
    \begin{tabular}{l l l}
        $\bm{\theta}$ 
        & 
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(1)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W_{z}^{(N)}\right)^T
            \odot 
            \bm{\alpha}_{z} \cdot \bm{x}_1
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W_z^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot
            \left[
                \bm{\beta}_z \odot W_z^{(1)} \bm{x}_1 
                +
                \bm{\alpha}_z
            \right]
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            b^{(1)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W_z^{(N)}\right)^T \odot \bm{\alpha}_z
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(W_z^{(N)}\right)^T 
            \odot
            \left[
                \bm{\beta}_z \odot W_z^{(1)}
            \right]
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            W^{(N)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \bm{\sigma}\left(W_z^{(1)}\bm{x}_1 + b_z^{(1)}\right)^T
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left(\bm{\alpha}_z \odot W_z^{(1)}\right)^T
        \end{aligned}$
        \\[0.5em] \hline
        \noalign{\vspace{0.5em}}
        $\begin{aligned}
            b^{(N)}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            1
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            0
        \end{aligned}$
    \end{tabular}
\end{center}

Für die Ableitungen von $M_{\bm{\theta}}= A(q)^2 + \epsilon$ und 
$M_{\bm{\theta}}' = 2A(q)s(q) \cdot z_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)$
nach den Parametern gilt:
% dM/dtheta dM'/dtheta
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial A(q)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z} 
    &=
    s(q) \cdot \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \\
    \frac{\partial s(q)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z} 
    &=
    s(q) \cdot (1-s(q)) \cdot \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \\
    \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z} 
    &=
    2A(q)
    \cdot 
    s(q)
    \cdot
    \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \\
    % Hier die Berechnung von dM'/dtheta:
    % mit Produktregel: (Asz') = dA/dtheta s z' + A ds/dtheta z' + As dz'/dtheta
    \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}'}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z} 
    &=
    2 \cdot 
    \left[
        \left(
            s(q)^2
            +
            A(q) 
            \cdot 
            s(q) \cdot (1-s(q))
        \right)
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}(q)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
        \cdot 
        z_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)
        +
        A(q)s(q)
        \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}'(q)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \right]
\end{align*} 

Für die Ableitung von $\bm{f}_2$ (\ref{eq:ELE_structured_L}) nach $\bm{\theta}_z$ gilt:
% df_2/dtheta_z
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    &=
    -\frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}^2} \cdot 
    \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \cdot 
    \left[
        -\frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}' \bm{x}_2^2 - V_{\bm{\theta}}' + u
    \right]
    +
    \frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}}
    \cdot 
    \left[
        -\frac{\bm{x}_2^2}{2} \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}'}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \right]
\end{align*}

Für die Jacobi-Matrix $\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial \bm{x}}$, wobei
$\bm{f}_1 = \bm{x}_2$ gilt.\footnote{
    Using quotient rule we get:
    %
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial \bm{x}_1}
        =
        \frac{
            \left[
                - \frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1) \bm{x}_2^2 
                - V_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1)
            \right]
            \cdot 
            M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)
            - 
            M_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)
            \cdot 
            \left[
                \bm{f}_2 \cdot M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)
            \right]
            }{
                M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)^2
            }
            =
            \frac{
                - \frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1) \bm{x}_2^2 
                - V_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1)
            }{
                M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)
            }
            -
            \frac{
                M_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)
            }{
                M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)
            }
            \cdot \bm{f}_2(\bm{x})
    \end{align*}
}
% f_x ausgerechnet
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial \bm{x}}
    &=
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \partial_{\bm{x}_1} \bm{f}_1 & \partial_{\bm{x}_2} \bm{f}_1
        \\
        \partial_{\bm{x}_1} \bm{f}_2 & \partial_{\bm{x}_2} \bm{f}_2
    \end{bmatrix}
    =
    \begin{bmatrix}
        0 & 1 \\
        - \frac{V_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1) + \frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1) \bm{x}_2^2}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)}
        - \bm{f}_2(\bm{x}) \frac{M_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)} 
        &
        - \frac{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)}{M_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)} \bm{x}_2
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

Die Kontraktion $\bm{f}_{\bm{x}}^T \cdot \bm{p}$ lautet:
% Kontraktion: f_x * p
\begin{align*}
    \bm{f}_{\bm{x}}^T \cdot \bm{p}
    &=
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \left(
            - \frac{V_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1) + \frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1) \bm{x}_2^2}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)}
            - \bm{f}_2(\bm{x}) \frac{M_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)} 
        \right)
        \cdot 
        \bm{p}_2(t)
        \\
        \bm{p}_1(t) + 
        \left(
            - \frac{M_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)} \bm{x}_2
        \right)
        \cdot 
        \bm{p}_2(t)
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

Die Adjungierte Gleichung ist gegeben durch
% p' = -H_x
\begin{align*}
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{\dot{p}}_1 \\
        \bm{\dot{p}}_2
    \end{bmatrix}
    =
    -2 \cdot
    \begin{bmatrix}
        q_{\bm{\theta}}(t) - q_{\text{data}}(t) \\
        0
    \end{bmatrix}
    -
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \left(
            - \frac{V_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1) + \frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}''(\bm{x}_1) \bm{x}_2^2}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)}
            - \bm{f}_2(\bm{x}) \frac{M_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)} 
        \right)
        \cdot 
        \bm{p}_2(t)
        \\
        \bm{p}_1(t) + 
        \left(
            - 
            \frac{
                M_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)
                }{
                    M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)
                } \bm{x}_2
        \right)
        \cdot 
        \bm{p}_2(t)
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

Die Kontraktion $\bm{f}_{\bm{\theta}}^T \cdot \bm{p}$ lautet:
% f_theta * p
\begin{align*}
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{0}_{1 \times (6h+2)} \\
        \partial_{\bm{\theta}} \bm{f}_2 
    \end{bmatrix}^T
    \cdot 
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{p}_1 \\
        \bm{p}_2
    \end{bmatrix}
    %
    &=
    \begin{bmatrix}
        - \frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{x}_1)} 
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial V_{\bm{\theta}}'(\bm{x}_1)}{\partial \bm{\theta}_V}
        \\
        -\frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}^2} \cdot 
        \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
        \cdot 
        \left[
            -\frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}' \bm{x}_2^2 - V_{\bm{\theta}}' + u
        \right]
        +
        \frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}}
        \cdot 
        \left[
            -\frac{\bm{x}_2^2}{2} \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}'}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
        \right]
    \end{bmatrix}^T
    \cdot \bm{p}_2(t)
\end{align*}

% Algorithmus - Adjoint Verfahren
\begin{algorithm*}\label{alg:LNN_structured}
    %\footnotesize
    \caption{LNN with a structured Lagrangian}
    \begin{algorithmic}[1]
        \State \textbf{Dataset}: $M$ Trajectories with $N$ time steps, $\bm{X}_0 \in \mathbb{R}^{2 \times M}$ initial values of the trajectoriess
        % Hier gehts ins Gradientenverfahren:
        \For {epoch in Episodes}
            %\State \textbf{Forward Pass}: Save $\bm{X} \in \mathbb{R}^{N\times 2 \times M}$
            \For {$i$ from $0$ to $N-2$}
                % Berechnung von Z und M
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{Z}_i 
                    &=
                    W_{z}^{(N)}
                    \log
                    \left(
                        \bm{1}
                        + 
                        \exp
                        \left(
                            W^{(1)}_z \bm{X}_{1,i} + b^{(1)}_z
                        \right)
                    \right)
                    +
                    b_z^{(N)}
                \end{aligned}$
                \Comment{Broadcast $b_z^{(N)}\cdot \bm{1}_M^T$}
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{A}_i 
                    &= 
                    \log
                    \left(
                        \bm{1}
                        + 
                        \exp
                        \left(
                            \bm{Z}_i
                        \right)
                    \right)
                \end{aligned}$
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{S}_i 
                    =
                    \frac{\bm{1}}{\bm{1} + \exp\left(- \bm{Z}_i\right)}
                \end{aligned}$
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{M}_i = \bm{A}_i^2 + \epsilon
                \end{aligned}$
                % Berechnung von alpha für z und V
                \State Compute $\bm{\alpha}_{\phi}$ for $\phi \in \{z,V\}$:
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{\alpha}_{\phi,i} 
                    &= 
                    \frac{
                        \exp\left(W^{(1)}_{\phi} \bm{X}_{i,1} + b^{(1)}_{\phi} \right)
                    }{
                        \bm{1}
                        + 
                        \exp
                        \left(
                            W^{(1)}_{\phi} \bm{X}_{i,1} + b^{(1)}_{\phi} 
                        \right)
                    }
                \end{aligned}$
                % Berechnung von beta für z und V
                \State Compute $\bm{\beta}_{\phi}$ for $\phi \in \{z,V\}$:
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{\beta}_{\phi,i} 
                    &= 
                    \bm{\alpha}_{\phi,i} 
                    \odot 
                    \left(
                        \bm{1} - \bm{\alpha}_{\phi,i} 
                    \right)
                \end{aligned}$
                % Berechnung von dZ/dq und d^2Z/dq^2
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{Z}_{i}' 
                    =
                    W_{z}^{(N)} 
                    \cdot 
                    \left(
                        \bm{\alpha}_{z,i} 
                        \odot
                        W_{z}^{(1)}
                    \right)
                    \quad
                    \bm{Z}_{i}''
                    =
                    W_{z}^{(N)} 
                    \cdot 
                    \left(
                        \bm{\beta}_{z,i} 
                        \odot
                        W_{z}^{(1)}
                        \odot
                        W_{z}^{(1)}
                    \right)
                \end{aligned}$
                % Berechnung von dM/dq 
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{M}_{i}'
                    =
                    2 \cdot \bm{A}_i \cdot \bm{S}_i \cdot \bm{Z}_{i}' 
                \end{aligned}$
                \Comment{Broadcast $W_{z}^{(1)}$ to $W_{z}^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T$}
                % Berechnung von d^2M/dq^2
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{M}_{i}''
                    =
                    2 \cdot 
                    \left(
                        \bm{S}_i^2 (\bm{Z}_{i}')^2 
                        + 
                        \bm{A}_i \cdot \bm{S}_i \cdot (1- \bm{S}_i)
                        \cdot 
                        (\bm{Z}_{i}')^2 
                        + 
                        \bm{A}_i \cdot \bm{S}_i \cdot \bm{Z}_{i}''
                    \right)
                \end{aligned}$
                % Berechnung dV/dq
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{V}_{i}'
                    =
                    W_{V}^{(N)} \cdot 
                    \left(
                        \bm{\alpha}_{i,V} \odot W_{V}^{(1)}
                    \right)
                \end{aligned}$
                % Berechnung d^2V/dq^2
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{V}_{i}''
                    =
                    W_{V}^{(N)} \cdot 
                    \left(
                        \bm{\beta}_{i,V} \odot W_{V}^{(1)} \odot W_{V}^{(1)}
                    \right)
                \end{aligned}$
                % Berechnung f_2 am Punkt X_i 
                \State Compute 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{f}_{i,2}
                    =
                    \frac{1}{\bm{M}_{i}} 
                    \cdot 
                    \left(
                        -\frac{1}{2} 
                        \bm{M}_{i}' \bm{X}_{i,2}^2 
                        - \bm{V}_{i}' 
                        + \bm{U}_i
                    \right)
                \end{aligned}$
                % Integration x'=f(x)
                \State Integration of the ODE:
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{\dot{X}}_{i} 
                    =
                    \begin{bmatrix}
                        \bm{X}_{i,2} \\
                        \frac{1}{\bm{M}_{i}} 
                        \cdot 
                        \left(
                            -\frac{1}{2} 
                            \bm{M}_{i}' \bm{X}_{i,2}^2 
                            - \bm{V}_{i}' 
                            + \bm{U}_i
                        \right)
                    \end{bmatrix}
                \end{aligned}$
            \EndFor
 
            \State Backward Integration:
            %Save $\bm{P} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times 2 \times M}$
            % Adjoint GLeichung
            $\begin{aligned}
                \bm{\dot{P}}_{i}
                =
                -2 
                \cdot 
                \begin{bmatrix}
                    \bm{X}_{i,1} - \bm{Q}_{i} \\
                    \bm{0}
                \end{bmatrix}
                -
                \begin{bmatrix}
                    \left(
                        -
                        \frac{
                            \bm{V}_{i}'' + \frac{1}{2} \bm{M}_{i}'' \bm{X}_{i,2}^2
                        }{\bm{M}_i}
                        - 
                        \bm{f}_{i,2} 
                        \cdot 
                        \frac{\bm{M}_{i}'}{\bm{M}_i}
                    \right)
                    \cdot 
                    \bm{P}_{i,2}
                    \\
                    \bm{P}_{i,1}
                    - 
                    \frac{\bm{M}_{i}'}{\bm{M}_i}
                    \bm{X}_{i,2}
                    \bm{P}_{i,2}
                \end{bmatrix}
            \end{aligned}$
            with $\bm{P}_{N-1} = \bm{0}$
            \State Gradient descent with 
            $\begin{aligned}
                \nabla_{\bm{\theta}} J 
                = 
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-1} \Delta t_i \cdot 
                \begin{bmatrix}
                    - \frac{1}{\bm{M}_{i}} \cdot 
                    \frac{\partial \bm{V}_{i}'}{\partial \bm{\theta}_V}
                    \\
                    -\frac{1}{\bm{M}_{i}^2} \cdot 
                    \frac{\partial \bm{M}_{i}}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
                    \cdot 
                    \left[
                        -\frac{1}{2} \bm{M}_{i}' \bm{x}_2^2 - \bm{V}_{i}' + \bm{U}_i
                    \right]
                    +
                    \frac{1}{\bm{M}_{i}}
                    \cdot 
                    \left[
                        -\frac{\bm{x}_2^2}{2} 
                        \frac{\partial \bm{M}_{i}'}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
                    \right]
                \end{bmatrix}
                \bm{P}_{i,2}^T
            \end{aligned}$
        \EndFor
        \State \textbf{Output}: Trained Parameters $\bm{\theta} = (W^{(1)}, b^{(1)}, W^{(N)}, b^{(N)})$
    \end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm*}


\subsection{Experimente}

Wir betrachten das Federpendel mit Steuerung $u$,\footnote{
    Hooke's Law states that the force of a spring is given by $F(q) = - k \cdot q$, 
    where $q$ denotes the displacement of the spring and $k$ is the spring constant. 
    The potential energy $V$ is defined by:
    %
    \begin{align*}
        - \frac{\partial V}{\partial q} = F(q) = - k \cdot q 
        \Rightarrow 
        V(q) = \frac{1}{2} k q^2 
    \end{align*}
    %
    The kinetic energy $T$ ist given by $T(\dot{q}) = \frac{1}{2} m \dot{q}^2$
}
dann lautet die Lagrange-Formulierung des Problems:
%
\begin{align*}
    \min_{q} J[q(t)] 
    =
    \min_{q}
    \int_{t_0}^{t_f} \frac{1}{2} m \cdot \dot{q}(t)^2 - \frac{1}{2} k q(t)^2 \dt
\end{align*}

% Erklärung des Federpendels:
\begin{figure}[ht]
    \begin{minipage}[c]{0.4\textwidth}
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[scale = 1.5, every node/.style={transform shape}]
            % Koordinatensystem
            %\draw[very thin, gray] (-5,0) grid (5,5); % Raster
            %\draw[->] (-5,0) -- (5,0); % x-Achse
            %\draw[->] (0,0) -- (0,5); % y-Achse
            
            % Befestigung Oben
            \fill[hellblau] (-1.0,4.0) rectangle (1.0,4.25);
            \draw[gray, line width = 0.2mm] (-1.0,4.0) rectangle (1.0,4.25);
            \draw[thick, hellblau] (0.0,4.0) -- (0.0,3.5);

            % Feder Malen
            \draw[thick, gray] (0.0,3.5) -- (0.25,3.4);
            \draw[thick, gray] (0.25,3.4) -- (-0.25,3.3); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (-0.25,3.3) -- (0.25,3.2); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (0.25,3.2) -- (-0.25,3.1); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (-0.25,3.1) -- (0.25,3.0); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (0.25,3.0) -- (-0.25,2.9); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (-0.25,2.9) -- (0.25,2.8); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (0.25,2.8) -- (-0.25,2.7); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (-0.25,2.7) -- (0.25,2.6); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (0.25,2.6) -- (-0.25,2.5); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (-0.25,2.5) -- (0.25,2.4); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (0.25,2.4) -- (-0.25,2.3); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (-0.25,2.3) -- (0.25,2.2); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (0.25,2.2) -- (-0.25,2.1); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (-0.25,2.1) -- (0.25,2.0); 
            \draw[thick, gray] (0.25,2.0) -- (0.0,1.9);

            \draw[thick, hellblau] (0.0,1.9) -- (0.0,1.5);
            \fill[dunkelrot] (-0.25,1.25) rectangle (0.25,1.5);
            \draw[gray, line width = 0.2mm] (-0.25,1.25) rectangle (0.25,1.5);

            % Kräfte
            %\draw[dunkelrot,thick, ->] (0.5,1.2) -- (0.5,1);
            %\draw[gruen,thick, ->] (0.5,1.3) -- (0.5,1.5);
            %\node at (0.75, 1.0) {\tiny $F_G$};
            %\node at (0.75, 1.5) {\tiny $F$};
        \end{tikzpicture}
    \end{minipage}%
    \hfill
    \begin{minipage}[c]{0.55\textwidth}
        Euler-Lagrange Gleichung des Systems lautet:
        % ELE: Federpendel
        \begin{align*}
            m \ddot{q}(t) + k q(t) = u(t) 
            \Leftrightarrow 
            \ddot{q}(t) = -\frac{k}{m} \cdot q(t) + \frac{1}{m} \cdot u(t)
        \end{align*}
        %
        Für den erweitereten Zustand $\bm{x} \in \mathbb{R}^2$ gilt:
        % x' = f(x,u)
        \begin{align*}
            \bm{x}(t) :=
            \begin{bmatrix}
                q(t) \\
                \dot{q}(t)
            \end{bmatrix}
            \quad
            \bm{\dot{x}}(t) 
            =
            \begin{bmatrix}
                0 & 1 \\
                -\frac{k}{m} & 0  
            \end{bmatrix}
            \cdot 
            \bm{x}(t)
            + 
            \begin{bmatrix}
                0 \\
                \frac{1}{m}
            \end{bmatrix}
            \cdot 
            u(t)
        \end{align*}
    \end{minipage}
    \caption{Federpendel mit Steuerung $u(t)$.}
    \label{fig:federpendel}
\end{figure}


% Wichtig: Wie garantieren wir, dass H>0 ist?
%--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Für die Wahl von $m=2$ und $k=0.5$ ohne externe Steuerung erhalten wir die Gleichung 
$\ddot{q}(t) + \frac{1}{4} q(t) = 0$.
Die analytische Lösung ist gegeben durch:
%
% Analytische Lösung für q(t): l^2 = -1/4 -> l = (+-) 1/2 i -> q1 = sin(1/2 t) q2= cos(1/2 t) 
\begin{align*}
    q(t) = 2 \dot{q}(0) \cdot \sin\left( \frac{t}{2} \right) + q(0) \cdot \cos\left(\frac{t}{2} \right)
\end{align*}

\begin{itemize}
    \item Wir erhalten somit mit der $\tanh$ Aktivierung
    %
    % alpha_i, beta_i , gamma_i für tanh im Batch Format:
    \begin{align*}
        % alpha_i
        \bm{\alpha}_i &= \bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{1}_M^T  - \bm{A}_i \odot \bm{A}_i \\
        % beta_i
        \bm{\beta}_i &= (-2) \cdot \bm{A}_i \odot \bm{\alpha}_i  \\
        % gamma_i
        \bm{\gamma}_i &= 2 \cdot \bm{\alpha}_i \odot \left(3 \cdot \bm{A}_i \odot \bm{A}_i - \bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right)
    \end{align*}
    %
    \item Für den Ausdruck für $\bm{H}$ gilt:
    %
    \begin{align*}
        \bm{H}_i^{(j)} =
        \sum_{k=1}^{h} W_{1k}^{(N)} \left[\bm{\beta}_i^{(j)}\right]_k 
        \underbrace{
            \left(W_{k2}^{(1)}\right)^2
        }_{\geq 0} 
        \quad
        \left[\bm{\beta}_i^{(j)}\right]_k = (-2) \cdot \tanh\left(\left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k \right) 
        \cdot \left(1 - \tanh\left(\left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k \right)\right) 
    \end{align*}
    %
    \item Mit den Initialisierungen
    %
    \begin{align*}
        W^{(1)}_{ij} \sim \mathsf{N}\left(0, 10^{-2} \right) 
        \quad
        q(0),\dot{q}(0) \sim \mathsf{U}\left[-\frac{3}{2}, \frac{3}{2}\right]
        \quad
        b^{(1)} = -1 
        \Rightarrow
        \left[\bm{z}_0^{(j)}\right]_k
        \approx \left(-4,2\right)
    \end{align*}
    %
    \item Für $\beta$ gilt allerdings
    %
    \begin{align}
        \beta(z<0) < 0  \quad \beta(z=0) = 0 \quad \beta(z > 0) > 0
    \end{align}
    \item Mit der $\mathsf{softplus}$ Aktivierung:
\end{itemize}


\begin{itemize}
    \item Konvergenz unter günstigen Bedingungen: 
    %
    %
    \item Divergenz bei negativer Massenmatrix: Bei 50 Seeds 
    %
    %
    \item Keine Identifikation der Lagrange-Funktion:
    %
    %
\end{itemize}

\section{Maximum Principle Model-based Learning}

%%%%
Follow \cite{PMP_controller} and \cite{HAC_controller} 
%

\begin{tabular}{lllll}
    Methode         & Critic            & Aktion    & Gradient & Modell \\ \hline
    PMP-Controller  & H (analytic)   & $\bm{u}$  & $\nabla_{\bm{u}} J = L_{\bm{u}} + \bm{p}^T \bm{f}_{\bm{u}}$ & LNN \\
    HAC             & H (analytic)   & $\pi_{\bm{\theta}}$ determ.  
        & $\int_{t_0}^{t_f} H_{\bm{u}} \frac{\partial \pi_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}$ 
        & LNN 
    \\
    SVG & H (analytic) & $\pi_{\bm{\theta}}$ stochast. & SVG Grad & LNN
    \\
    DPG & $Q_{\Phi}$ & $\pi_{\bm{\theta}}$ & Kettenregel durch Q & Simulation 
    \\
    Reinforce &  Monte Carlo: $R_t$ &  $\pi_{\bm{\theta}}$ stochast. & Score Function & Simulation
