        \frac{\partial V}{\partial b^{(N)}}
        &=
        1
        \\
        \frac{\partial V}{\partial \bm{b}^{(k)}}
        &=
        \frac{\partial V}{\partial \bm{a}^{(k)}} 
        \cdot
        \frac{\partial \bm{a}^{(k)}}{\partial \bm{z}^{(k)}}
        \cdot
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{z}^{(k)}}{\partial \bm{b}^{(k)}}
        }_{
            I_n
        }
        =
        \bm{\delta}^{(k)}
        \quad
        k = 1, \cdots , L
    \end{align*}
\end{beweis}

%\begin{algorithm}
%    \caption{Backpropagation in Neural Networks $2$ Hidden-Layers}
%    \begin{algorithmic}
%        \State Given $\bm{W}^{(1)},\bm{W}^{(2)}, \bm{W}^{(N)} , \bm{b}^{(1)}, \bm{b}^{(2)}, b^{(N)}, 
%        \begin{bmatrix} t_i & X_{t_i} \end{bmatrix}^T$ 
%
%        \State Computation Outer-Layer
%        %
%        \begin{align*}
%            t
%        \end{align*}
%    \end{algorithmic}
%\end{algorithm}

% Ableitung nach dem Input beim one Hidden-Layer
\begin{satz}
    Sei $V:\mathbb{R}^{\text{Input}} \to \mathbb{R}$ ein neuronales Netz mit Input $\bm{x}$. 
    Dann gilt:
    %
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{\partial V}{\partial \bm{x}} = \bm{\delta}^{(1)} \cdot \bm{W}^{(1)}
        \quad
        \nabla_{\bm{x}} V(\bm{x}) 
        =
        \left(\bm{\delta}^{(1)} \cdot \bm{W}^{(1)}\right)^T 
    \end{align*}
\end{satz}

% Herleitung dazu aus Kettenregel
\begin{beweis}
    Es gilt nach der Kettenregel:
    %
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{\partial V}{\partial \bm{x}}
        &=
        \frac{\partial V}{\partial \bm{z}^{(1)}}
        \cdot
        \frac{\partial \bm{z}^{(1)}}{\partial \bm{x}}
        = 
        \bm{\delta}^{(1)} \cdot \bm{W}^{(1)}
    \end{align*}

    Für $L$ Hidden-Layer erhalten wir mittels Backpropagation
    %
    \begin{align*}
        \bm{\delta}^{(L)} = \bm{W}^{(N)} \circ \left[1 - \left(\bm{a}^{(L)}\right)^2 \right]
        \quad
        \bm{\delta}^{(k)} 
        = 
        \left(
            \bm{\delta}^{(k+1)} \cdot \bm{W}^{(k+1)}
        \right) 
        \circ  
        \left[
            1 - \left(\bm{a}^{(k)}\right)^2 
        \right],
        \quad
        k=L-1, \cdots , 1
    \end{align*}
\end{beweis}

Unser adjungiertes System lautet:
% Adjungierte Gleichung + Zustandsgleichung
\begin{align*}
    \bm{\dot{p}}(t) 
    &=
    - \bm{p}(t) \cdot 
    \left[
        \textsf{diag}
        \left(
            \vphantom{\int}
            1 - 
            \tanh
            \left(
                \sum_{j=1}^{n}
                \bm{W}_{ij} \bm{a}_j(t) + \bm{b}_{i}
            \right)^2
        \right)_{i=1,\cdots , n}
        \cdot
        \bm{W}(t)
        - \bm{I}_n
    \right]
    \quad
    \bm{p}(T) = 
    \bm{W}^N
    \\
    \bm{\dot{a}}(t)
    &=
    \tanh
        \left(
            \sum_{j=1}^{n}
            \bm{W}_{ij} \bm{a}_j(t) + \bm{b}_{i}
        \right)_{i=1,\cdots , n}
    - \bm{a}(t)
    \quad
    \bm{a}(0) = \bm{x}_0
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitung der Ausgabe $\Phi = \bm{W}^{(N)} \cdot \bm{a}(T) + b^N$ gilt: 
%
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \Phi}{\partial \bm{u}} 
    &=
    \int_{0}^{T} \bm{p}(t) \cdot \bm{f}_{\bm{u}}(\bm{a}(t), \bm{u}) \dt
    \quad
    \frac{\partial \Phi}{\partial \bm{W}^{(N)}}=\bm{a}(T)
    \quad
    \frac{\partial \Phi}{\partial b^{(N)}}=1
\end{align*}

Es gilt für die Komponente $i$
%
\begin{align*}
    \bm{f}_{i}(\bm{a}(t), \bm{u})
    &=
    \tanh
    \left[
        \sum_{k=1}^{n} \bm{W}_{ik} \bm{a}_k(t) + \bm{b}_{i}
    \right]
    -
    \bm{a}_i(t)
\end{align*}

Für die partielle Ableitung nach $\bm{a}_j$ und die Jacobi-Matrix $\bm{f}_{\bm{a}}$ erhalten wir
%
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial \bm{a}_j}
    &=
    \left[
        1 - 
        \tanh
        \left(
            \sum_{k=1}^{n} \bm{W}_{ik} \bm{a}_k(t) + \bm{b}_{i}
        \right)^2
    \right] 
    \cdot
    \bm{W}_{ij} 
    -  
    \frac{\partial \bm{a}_i (t)}{\partial \bm{a}_j (t)}
    \\
    \left(
        \vphantom{\int}
        \bm{f}_{\bm{a}}(\bm{a}(t), \bm{u})
    \right)_{ij}
    &=
    \left[
        1 - 
        \tanh
        \left(
            \sum_{k=1}^{n} \bm{W}_{ik} \bm{a}_k(t) + \bm{b}_{i}
        \right)^2
    \right] 
    \cdot
    \bm{W}_{ij}
    - \delta_{ij}
\end{align*}

Für die partiellen Ableitungen nach den Parametern $\bm{W}$ und $\bm{b}$ gilt:
%
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial \bm{W}_{lj}} 
    &=
    \begin{cases}
        \left[
            1 - 
            \tanh
            \left(
                \vphantom{\int}
                \sum_{k=1}^{n} \bm{W}_{ik} \bm{a}_k(t) + \bm{b}_{i}
            \right)^2
        \right] \cdot \bm{a}_j(t) , & i = l \\
        0, & i \neq l
    \end{cases}
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial \bm{b}_{j}} 
    &=
    \begin{cases}
        1 - 
        \tanh
        \left(
            \vphantom{\int}
            \sum_{k=1}^{n} \bm{W}_{ik} \bm{a}_k(t) + \bm{b}_{i}
        \right)^2 , & i = j \\
        0, & i \neq j
    \end{cases}
\end{align*}

Diskretisierung mittels Schrittweite $h=1$ und $\bm{u}(t) = (\bm{W}(t), \bm{b}(t))$:
% a' = f(a,u)
\begin{align*}
    \bm{a}(t_k) 
    &= 
    \bm{a}(t_{k-1}) + \bm{f}(\bm{a}(t_{k-1}), \bm{u}(t_{k-1})) 
    = 
    \tanh
    \left[
        \vphantom{\int}
        \bm{W}(t_{k-1}) \cdot \bm{a}(t_{k-1}) + \bm{b}(t_{k-1})
    \right]  
    \\
    \bm{a}(t_k) &:= \bm{a}^{(k)} 
    \quad
    \bm{a}^{(0)} = \bm{x}_0
    \quad
    \bm{W}(t_{k-1}) := \bm{W}^{k} 
    \quad
    \bm{b}(t_{k-1}) := \bm{b}^{(k)}
    \\
    \bm{a}^{(k)}
    &=
    \tanh
    \left[
        \vphantom{\int}
        \bm{W}^{(k)} \cdot \bm{a}^{(k-1)} + \bm{b}^{(k)}
    \right]
    :=
    \bm{F}_k (\bm{a}^{(k-1)}, \bm{u}^{(k)})
\end{align*}

Für eine Diskretisierung $\left\{t_0 , \cdots ,t_L =T \right\}$ erhalten wir somit das Neuronale Netz
% NN 
\begin{align*}
    \bm{a}^{(0)} &= \bm{x}_0
    \\
    \bm{a}^{(k)} &= 
    \tanh
    \left[
        \bm{W}^{(k)}\bm{a}^{(k-1)} + \bm{b}^{(k)}
    \right]
    =: \bm{F}_k(\bm{a}^{(k-1)}, \bm{u}^{(k)})
    \quad
    k = 1,\dots, L
    \\
    a^{(N)} &= \Phi\left(\bm{a}^{(L)}\right) := \bm{W}^{(N)}\bm{a}^{(L)} + b^{(N)}
\end{align*}

Für die adjungierte Gleichung erhalten wir:
% p' = -H_a
\begin{align*}
    \bm{p}(t_{k}) &= \bm{p}(t_{k-1}) - \bm{p}(t_{k}) 
    \cdot \bm{f}_{\bm{a}}(\bm{a}(t_{k-1}),\bm{u}(t_{k-1}))
    \\
    \bm{p}^{(k-1)} 
    &= 
    \bm{p}^{(k)} 
    + 
    \bm{p}^{(k)}
    \cdot
    \textsf{diag}
    \left[
        \vphantom{\int}
        1 - 
        \tanh
        \left(
            \vphantom{\int}
            \bm{W}^{(k)} \cdot \bm{a}^{(k-1)} + \bm{b}^{(k)}
        \right)_i^2
    \right]_{i=1,\cdots , n}
    \cdot
    \bm{W}^{(k)}
    - 
    \bm{p}^{(k)}
    \cdot 
    \bm{I}_n
    \\
    \bm{p}^{(k-1)} 
    &=
    \bm{p}^{(k)}
    \cdot
    \frac{\partial \bm{F}_k}{\partial \bm{a}^{(k-1)}}
    \quad
    \bm{p}^{(L)} = \frac{\partial \Phi}{\partial \bm{a}^{(L)}} = \bm{W}^{(N)}
\end{align*}

Das Maximumprinzip des Steuerungproblems lautet dann: 
%
\begin{align*}
    H_k \left(\bm{a}^{(k-1)}, \bm{u}^{(k)}, \bm{p}^{(k)}\right) 
    &= 
    \bm{p}^{(k)} \cdot \bm{F}_k(\bm{a}^{(k-1)}, \bm{u}^{(k)})
    \\
    \frac{\partial J}{\partial \bm{u}^{(k)}} 
    &= 
    \frac{\partial H_k}{\partial \bm{u}^{(k)}} 
    = \bm{p}^{(k)} \cdot \frac{\partial \bm{F}_k}{\partial \bm{u}^{(k)}}, 
    \quad 
    k = 1,\dots,L.
    \\
    \frac{\partial J}{\partial \bm{W}^{(N)}} 
    &=
    (\bm{a}^{(L)})^T
    \quad
    \frac{\partial J}{\partial b^{(N)}} = 1
\end{align*}


Mit $\bm{a}^{(k)} = \tanh(\bm{W}^{(k)}\bm{a}^{(k-1)} + \bm{b}^{(k)})$ erhalten wir:
%
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{F}_k}{\partial \bm{a}^{(k-1)}} 
    &=
    \operatorname{diag} \left[1- \left( \bm{a}^{(k)} \right)^2 \right] \cdot \bm{W}^{(k)}
    \\
    \bm{p}^{(k-1)} 
    &=
    \bm{p}^{(k)} 
    \cdot 
    \frac{\partial \bm{F}_k}{\partial \bm{a}^{(k-1)}} 
    \\
    \bm{p}^{(k-1)} 
    &= 
    \underbrace{
        \bm{p}^{(k)} 
        \circ 
        \operatorname{diag} \left[1- \left( \bm{a}^{(k)} \right)^2 \right]
    }_{
        := \bm{\delta}^{(k)}
    } 
    \cdot \bm{W}^{(k)}
        \\
    \underbrace{
        \bm{p}^{(k-1)} \circ \left[1- \left( \bm{a}^{(k-1)} \right)^2 \right]
    }_{
        \bm{\delta}^{(k-1)}
    }
    &=
    \bm{\delta}^{(k)} \cdot \bm{W}^{(k)} \circ \left[1- \left( \bm{a}^{(k-1)} \right)^2 \right]
\end{align*}


Wobei $\left[1- \left( \bm{a}^{(k)} \right)^2 \right]$ der Vektor 
mit den Elementen $(1-\bm{a}^{(k)}_i)_{i=1,\cdots , n}$ bezeichnet.
Für die Hidden-Layers gilt entsprechend
%
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial J}{\partial \bm{W}_{ij}^{(k)}}
    &=
    \sum_{m} \bm{p}_m^{(k)} \cdot 
    \frac{\partial (\bm{F}_k)_m}{\partial \bm{W}_{ij}^{(k)}}
    \quad
    (\bm{F}_k)_m = 
    \tanh
    \left(
        \sum_{j} \bm{W}_{mj}^{(k)} \cdot \bm{a}^{(k-1)}_j + \bm{b}^{(k)}_m
    \right)
    \\
    &=
    \sum_{m = i} \bm{p}_m^{(k)} \cdot 
    \left[
        1 - 
        \tanh
        \left(
            \sum_{j} \bm{W}_{ij}^{(k)} \cdot \bm{a}^{(k-1)}_j + \bm{b}^{(k)}_m
        \right)^2
    \right]
    \cdot 
    \bm{a}_j^{(k-1)}
    \\
    &=
    \underbrace{
        \bm{p}_i^{(k)} \cdot 
        \left[
            1 - 
            \left(
                \bm{a}_i^{(k)}
            \right)^2
        \right]
    }_{
        \bm{\delta}^{(k)}_i
    }
    \cdot 
    \bm{a}_j^{(k-1)}
\end{align*}

Als Matrix erhalten wir somit erneut 
%
\begin{align*}
     \frac{\partial J}{\partial \bm{W}^{(k)}} 
     = (\bm{\delta}^{(k)})^T (\bm{a}^{(k-1)})^T 
     := \bm{\delta}^{(k)} \otimes \bm{a}^{(k-1)}
\end{align*}

Für den Bias gilt analog 
%
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial J}{\partial \bm{b}_{i}^{(k)}}
    =
    \sum_{m} \bm{p}_m^{(k)} \cdot 
    \frac{\partial (\bm{F}_k)_m}{\partial \bm{b}_{i}^{(k)}}
    =
    \bm{p}_i^{(k)} \cdot
    \left[
        1 - 
        \left(
            \bm{a}_i^{(k)}
        \right)^2
    \right]
    \Rightarrow
    \frac{\partial J}{\partial \bm{b}^{(k)}} = \bm{\delta}^{(k)}
\end{align*}

\begin{algorithm}
    \caption{Backpropagation in Neural Networks $1$ Hidden-Layers}
    \begin{algorithmic}
        \State Given $\bm{W}^{(1)}, \bm{W}^{(N)} , \bm{b}^{(1)}, b^{(N)}, 
        \begin{bmatrix} t_i & X_{t_i} \end{bmatrix}^T$ 

        \State Foward Computation
        %
        \begin{align*}
            \bm{z}^{(1)} &= \bm{W}^{(1)} \cdot 
            \begin{bmatrix}
                t_i \\
                X_{t_i}
            \end{bmatrix}
            + 
            \bm{b}^{(1)}
            \\
            \bm{a}^{(1)} &= \tanh\left(\bm{z}^{(1)}\right)
            \\
            V 
            &=
            \bm{W}^{(N)} \cdot \bm{a}^{(1)} + b^{(N)}
        \end{align*}
        %
        \State Backpropagation
        %
        \begin{align*}
            \bm{\delta}^{(1)} &=
            \bm{W}^{(N)} \circ 
            \left[
                1 - \left( \bm{a}^{(1)} \right)^2
            \right]
            \\
            \frac{\partial V}{\partial \bm{W}^{(N)}}
            &=
            \bm{a}^{(1)}
            \\
            \frac{\partial V}{\partial b_N} 
            &= 1
            \\
            \frac{\partial V}{\partial \bm{W}^{(1)}}
            &=
            \left(\bm{\delta}^{(1)} \right)^T
            \cdot
            \begin{bmatrix}
                t_i & X_{t_i}
            \end{bmatrix}
            \\
            \frac{\partial V}{\partial \bm{b}^{(1)}}
            &=
            \left(\bm{\delta}^{(1)} \right)^T
        \end{align*}

        \Return 
        %
        \begin{align*}
            \nabla_\phi V =
            \begin{bmatrix}
                \left(\bm{\delta}^{(1)} \right)^T \cdot t_i \\
                \left(\bm{\delta}^{(1)} \right)^T \cdot X_{t_i}
                \\
                \left(\bm{\delta}^{(1)}\right)^T
                \\
                \bm{a}^{(1)}
                \\
                1
            \end{bmatrix}
            \quad
            \phi
            =
            \begin{bmatrix}
                \bm{W}^{(1)} \cdot \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 0 \end{bmatrix}^T \\
                \bm{W}^{(1)}  \cdot \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}^T \\
                \bm{b}^{(1)} \\
                \left(\bm{W}^{(N)}\right)^T \\
                b^{(N)}
            \end{bmatrix}
        \end{align*}
    \end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

\section{Algorithmen}

\begin{algorithm}\label{alg:MLP_Sensitivity_full}
    \caption{Training of a MLP Neural ODE with Sensitivity Approach}
    \begin{algorithmic}[1]
        \State \textbf{Dataset}: $M$ Trajectories with $N$ time steps. 
        Where $\bm{X}_0 \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times M}$ are the initial values 
        \State Set $\bm{X}_0$ as $\left[\bm{x}_{0, \text{data}}^{(j)}\right]_{j=0,\cdots , M-1} $
        % Hier gehts ins Gradientenverfahren:
        \For {epoch in Episodes}
            \State \textbf{Forward Pass}: Save the Trajectories $\bm{X}_i, \bm{A}_i, \bm{\alpha}_i$
            \For {$i=0$ to $N-1$} \Comment{Forward Pass}
                \State             
                $\begin{aligned}%[t]
                    \bm{Z}_i &=W^{(1)} \bm{X}_i + b^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T
                    \\
                    \bm{A}_i &=\tanh\left(\bm{Z}_i\right)
                    \\
                    \bm{\alpha}_{i} &= \bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{1}_M^T - \bm{A}_{i} \odot \bm{A}_{i} 
                    \\
                    \bm{X}_{i+1} &= \bm{X}_{i} + \Delta t_i \cdot \left(W^{(N)} \bm{A}_i + b^{(N)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
                \end{aligned}$
            \EndFor
            \State 
            \State \textbf{Sensitivity ODE}: Save the Compututed Sensitivities $\bm{S}_{\theta}$ \Comment{Sensitivity}
            \For {$j=0$ to $M-1$}
                \For {$i=0$ to $N-1$} 
                    \State 
                    $\begin{aligned}
                        \bm{f}_{\bm{x},i}^{(j)}
                        &=
                        W^{(N)} \mathsf{diag}\left(\bm{\alpha}_i^{(j)}\right) W^{(1)}
                        \\
                        \left[\bm{S}_{W^{(1)}}^{(j)}\right]_{i+1} 
                        &=
                        % Rekursion für W^1
                        \left[\bm{S}_{W^{(1)}}^{(j)}\right]_{i} 
                        +
                        \Delta t_i
                        \cdot
                        \bm{f}_{\bm{x},i}^{(j)}
                        \left[\bm{S}_{W^{(1)}}^{(j)}\right]_i 
                        +
                        \Delta t_i
                        \cdot
                        \left[
                            W^{(N)}  \mathsf{diag}(\bm{\alpha}_i^{(j)})
                        \right] 
                        \otimes 
                        \left(\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right)^T
                        \\
                        \left[\bm{S}_{b^{(1)}}^{(j)}\right]_{i+1} 
                        &= 
                        % Rekursion für b^1
                        \left[\bm{S}_{b^{(1)}}^{(j)}\right]_{i} 
                        +
                        \Delta t_i
                        \cdot
                        \bm{f}_{\bm{x},i}^{(j)}
                        \left[\bm{S}_{b^{(1)}}^{(j)}\right]_i
                        +
                        \Delta t_i
                        \cdot
                        W^{(N)} \mathsf{diag}\left(\bm{\alpha}_i^{(j)}\right)
                        \\
                        \left[\bm{S}_{W^{(N)}}^{(j)}\right]_{i+1} 
                        &= 
                        % Rekursion für W^N
                        \left[\bm{S}_{W^{(N)}}^{(j)}\right]_{i} 
                        +
                        \Delta t_i
                        \cdot
                        \bm{f}_{\bm{x},i}^{(j)}
                        \left[\bm{S}_{W^{(N)}}^{(j)}\right]_i
                        +
                        \Delta t_i
                        \cdot
                        \bm{I}_n \otimes \left(\bm{A}_i^{(j)}\right)^T
                        \\
                        \left[\bm{S}_{b^{(N)}}^{(j)}\right]_{i+1} 
                        &= 
                        % Rekursion für b^N
                        \left[\bm{S}_{b^{(N)}}^{(j)}\right]_{i} 
                        +
                        \Delta t_i
                        \cdot
                        \bm{f}_{\bm{x},i}^{(j)}
                        \left[\bm{S}_{b^{(N)}}^{(j)}\right]_i
                        +
                        \Delta t_i \cdot \bm{I}_n
                    \end{aligned}$
                \EndFor
            \EndFor
            \State 
            \State \textbf{Gradient Computation}: For each $\theta$ a unique Gradient will be computed
            \State 
            $\begin{aligned}
                \nabla_{\theta} J 
                = 
                \frac{1}{N M} \sum_{i=0}^{N-1} \Delta t_i \sum_{j=0}^{M-1} 
                2 \left(\bm{X}_i^{(j)} - \bm{X}_{i, \text{data}}^{(j)}\right)^T  
                \left[\bm{S}_{\theta}^{(j)}\right]_i 
            \end{aligned}$
            \State Update the Parameter $\theta$ using gradient descent
        \EndFor
        \State \textbf{Output}: Trained Parameters $\theta = (W^{(1)}, b^{(1)}, W^{(N)}, b^{(N)})$
    \end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

\begin{algorithm}\label{alg:MLP_Adjoint_full}
    \caption{Training of a MLP Neural ODE with Adjoint Approach}
    \begin{algorithmic}[1]
        \State \textbf{Dataset}: $M$ Trajectories with $N$ time steps. 
        Where $\bm{X}_0 \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times M}$ are the initial values 
        \State Set $\bm{X}_0$ as $\left[\bm{x}_{0, \text{data}}^{(j)}\right]_{j=0,\cdots , M-1} $
        % Hier gehts ins Gradientenverfahren:
        \For {epoch in Episodes}
            \State \textbf{Forward Pass}: Save the Trajectories $\bm{X}_i, \bm{A}_i, \bm{\alpha}_i$
            \For {$i=0$ to $N-1$} \Comment{Forward Pass}
                \State             
                $\begin{aligned}%[t]
                    \bm{Z}_i &=W^{(1)} \bm{X}_i + b^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T
                    \\
                    \bm{A}_i &=\tanh\left(\bm{Z}_i\right)
                    \\
                    \bm{\alpha}_{i} &= \bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{1}_M^T - \bm{A}_{i} \odot \bm{A}_{i} 
                    \\
                    \bm{X}_{i+1} &= \bm{X}_{i} + \Delta t_i \cdot \left(W^{(N)} \bm{A}_i + b^{(N)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
                \end{aligned}$
            \EndFor
            \State 
            \State \textbf{Backward Pass}: Using the terminal condition $\bm{P}_{N-1} = \bm{0}_{n \times M}$ \Comment{Adjoint}
            \For {$i=N-2$ to $0$} 
                \State 
                $\begin{aligned}
                    \bm{\delta}_{i+1}
                    &=
                    \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T \cdot \bm{P}_{i+1}
                    \\
                    \bm{P}_i 
                    &=
                    \bm{P}_{i+1} 
                    + \Delta t_i \cdot 
                    \left\{
                        2 \cdot \left(\bm{X}_{i+1} - \bm{X}_{i+1}^{(data)}\right)
                        +
                        \left(W^{(1)}\right)^T 
                        \cdot
                        \left[
                            \bm{\alpha}_{i+1}
                            \odot 
                            \bm{\delta}_{i+1}
                        \right]
                    \right\} 
                \end{aligned}$
            \EndFor
            \State Set $\bm{delta}_0 = \left(W^{(N)}\right)^T \cdot \bm{P}_0$
            \State \textbf{Gradient Computation}: 
            \State 
            $\begin{aligned}
                \nabla_{W^{(1)}} J(\bm{u})
                &=
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-2} \Delta t_i \cdot 
                \sum_{j=0}^{M-1}
                \left[
                    \underbrace{
                        \bm{\alpha}_i^{(j)} \odot \bm{\delta}_i^{(j)}
                    }_{h \times 1} 
                \right] 
                \cdot \underbrace{\left(\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right)^T}_{1 \times n}
                =
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-2} \Delta t_i \cdot 
                \left[
                    \underbrace{
                        \bm{\alpha}_i \odot \bm{\delta}_i
                    }_{h \times M} 
                \right]
                \cdot \underbrace{\left(\bm{X}_i\right)^T}_{M \times n}
                \\
                \nabla_{b^{(1)}} J(\bm{u})
                &=
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-2} \Delta t_i \cdot
                \sum_{j=0}^{M-1}
                \left[
                    \underbrace{\bm{\alpha}_i^{(j)} \odot \bm{\delta}_i^{(j)}}_{h \times 1}
                \right] 
                =
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-2} \Delta t_i \cdot
                \left[
                    \underbrace{\bm{\alpha}_i \odot \bm{\delta}_i}_{h \times M}
                \right] 
                \cdot \bm{1}_{M}
                \\
                \nabla_{W^{(N)}} J(\bm{u})
                &=
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-2} \Delta t_i \cdot
                \sum_{j=0}^{M-1}
                \left[
                    \underbrace{\bm{P}_i^{(j)} \cdot \bm{A}_i^{(j)T}}_{n \times h} 
                \right] 
                = 
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-2} \Delta t_i \cdot \underbrace{\bm{P}_i \cdot \left(\bm{A}_i\right)^T}_{n \times h}
                \\
                \nabla_{b^{(N)}} J(\bm{u})
                &=
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-2} \Delta t_i \cdot
                \sum_{j=0}^{M-1}
                \underbrace{\bm{P}_i^{(j)}}_{n \times 1}
                =
                \sum_{i=0}^{N-2} \Delta t_i \cdot 
                \underbrace{\bm{P}_i}_{n \times M} 
                \cdot 
                \underbrace{\bm{1}_M}_{M \times 1}
            \end{aligned}$
            \State Update the Parameter $\theta$ using gradient descent
        \EndFor
        \State \textbf{Output}: Trained Parameters $\theta = (W^{(1)}, b^{(1)}, W^{(N)}, b^{(N)})$
    \end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

\section{Herleitungen}

\subsection{Neural ODEs}
% W_{jk} steckt nur in einer komponente von f drin 
Für die Jacobi-Matrix 
$\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial \bm{u}}: \mathbb{R}^{m} \to \mathbb{R}^n$ gilt:
% f_u
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial \bm{u}} 
    =
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(1)}}
        }_{
            n \times (h \cdot n)
        } 
        &
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial b^{(1)}}
        }_{
            n \times h
        }
        &
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(N)}}
        }_{
            n \times (n \cdot h)
        }
        &
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial b^{(N)}}
        }_{
            n \times n
        } 
    \end{pmatrix}
\end{align*}

Die konkreten Matrizen werden konkret spaltenweise gebaut, d.h. für die $i-$te Zeile 
von $\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(1)}}$:
% df/dW^1 in der i-ten Zeile
\begin{align*}
    \left(
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{11}} 
        \cdots
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h1}}
        }_{
            1.\text{ Spalte von } W^{(1)}
        }
        \bigg| 
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{12}} 
            \cdots
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h2}}
        }_{
            2.\text{ Spalte von } W^{(1)}
        }
        \bigg|
        \cdots
        \bigg|
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{1n}} 
            \cdots
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{hn}}
        }_{
            n \text{ Spalte von } W^{(1)}
        }
    \right)
    \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times (h \cdot n)}
\end{align*}

Transponieren wir die gesamte Matrix 
$\left(\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(1)}}\right)^T$ so erhalten wir für 
$\left(\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(1)}}\right)^T \cdot \bm{p}(t)$:
% (df/dW^1)^T p(t)
\begin{align*}
    \left(
        \begin{array}{cccc}
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial W^{(1)}_{11}}  
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial W^{(1)}_{11}} 
            &
            \cdots
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial W^{(1)}_{11}} 
            \\
            \vdots & \vdots & & \vdots
            \\
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h1}}
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h1}}
            &
            \cdots
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h1}}
            \\[0.3cm]
            \cline{1-2}
            \cline{4-4}
            \noalign{\vspace{0.3cm}}
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial W^{(1)}_{12}}
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial W^{(1)}_{12}}
            &
            \cdots
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial W^{(1)}_{12}}
            \\
            \vdots & \vdots & & \vdots
            \\
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h2}}
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h2}}
            &
            \cdots
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h2}}
            \\[0.3cm]
            \cline{1-2}
            \cline{4-4}
            \noalign{\vspace{0.1cm}}
            \vdots 
            &
            \vdots
            &
            &
            \vdots
            \\
            \cline{1-2}
            \cline{4-4}
            \noalign{\vspace{0.3cm}}
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial W^{(1)}_{1n}}
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial W^{(1)}_{1n}} 
            &
            \cdots
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial W^{(1)}_{1n}}
            \\
            \vdots 
            &
            \vdots
            &
            &
            \vdots
            \\
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial W^{(1)}_{hn}}
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial W^{(1)}_{hn}}
            &
            \cdots
            &
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial W^{(1)}_{hn}}
        \end{array}
    \right)
    \cdot
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \bm{p}_1(t) \\
        \bm{p}_2(t) \\
        \vdots \\
        \bm{p}_n(t)
    \end{pmatrix}
    =
    \left(
        \begin{array}{c}
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{11}} \bm{p}_i(t)
            \\
            \vdots
            \\
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h1}} \bm{p}_i(t) \\[0.3cm] \hline
            \noalign{\vspace{0.3cm}}
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{12}} \bm{p}_i(t)
            \\
            \vdots
            \\
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{h2}} \bm{p}_i(t) \\[0.3cm] \hline
            \noalign{\vspace{0.1cm}}
            \vdots \\[0.1cm] \hline
            \noalign{\vspace{0.3cm}}
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{1n}} \bm{p}_i(t) \\
            \vdots \\
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{hn}} \bm{p}_i(t)
        \end{array}
    \right)
    =
    \left(
        \begin{array}{c}
            \bm{\alpha}_1 \bm{\delta}_1 \bm{x}_1
            \\
            \vdots
            \\
            \bm{\alpha}_h \bm{\delta}_h \bm{x}_1 \\[0.3cm] \hline
            \bm{\alpha}_1 \bm{\delta}_1 \bm{x}_2 
            \\
            \vdots
            \\
            \bm{\alpha}_h \bm{\delta}_h \bm{x}_2 \\[0.3cm] \hline
            \noalign{\vspace{0.1cm}}
            \vdots \\[0.1cm] \hline
            \noalign{\vspace{0.3cm}}
            \bm{\alpha}_1 \bm{\delta}_1 \bm{x}_n 
            \\
            \vdots
            \\
            \bm{\alpha}_h \bm{\delta}_h \bm{x}_n
        \end{array}
    \right)
\end{align*}

Für die einzelne Komponente gilt nun: 
% Komponentenweise Betrachtung von: (df/dW^1)^T p(t)
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(1)}_{\blau{j}k}} \cdot \bm{p}_i(t)
    =
    \sum_{i=1}^{n} \bm{\alpha}_{\blau{j}} W^{(N)}_{i \blau{j}} \bm{p}_i(t) \bm{x}_k 
    =
    \bm{\alpha}_{\blau{j}}  \cdot 
    \left( \underbrace{\left(W^{(N)}\right)^T \cdot \bm{p}(t)}_{:= \bm{\delta}} \right)_{\blau{j}}
    \cdot \bm{x}_k
    =
    \left(\bm{\alpha} \odot \bm{\delta}\right)_{\blau{j}} \cdot \bm{x}_k
\end{align*}

Für den Beitrag zum Gradienten erhalten wir: 
% J_W1
\begin{align*}
    \nabla_{W^{(1)}} J(\bm{u})
    =
    \left(\bm{\alpha} \odot \bm{\delta}\right) \cdot \bm{x}^T
    \in \mathbb{R}^{h \times n}
\end{align*}
% Hier nun Zerlegen \frac{df_i}{dW^1_{jk}} = delta_j * alpha_j * x_k und 
% delta * (hadamard) alpha = gamma 

Für die $i$-te Zeile von $\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(N)}}$ gilt:
%
\begin{align*}
    \left(
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{11}} 
        \cdots
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{n1}}
        }_{
            1.\text{ Spalte von } W^{(N)}
        }
        \bigg| 
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{12}} 
            \cdots
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{n2}}
        }_{
            2.\text{ Spalte von } W^{(N)}
        }
        \bigg|
        \cdots
        \bigg|
        \underbrace{
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{1h}} 
            \cdots
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{nh}}
        }_{
            h \text{ Spalte von } W^{(N)}
        }
    \right)
    \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times (n \cdot h)}
\end{align*}

Aufstellen der Matrix $\left(\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(N)}}\right)^T \bm{p}$:
%df/dW^N
\begin{align*}
    \left(\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(N)}}\right)^T \cdot \bm{p}(t)
    &=
    \left(
        \underbrace{
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{11}} \cdot \bm{p}_i 
            \cdots
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{n1}} \cdot \bm{p}_i
        }_{
            1.\text{ Spalte von } W^{(N)}
        }
        \bigg|
        \cdots
        \bigg|
        \underbrace{
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{1h}} \cdot \bm{p}_i 
            \cdots
            \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{nh}} \cdot \bm{p}_i 
        }_{
            h \text{ Spalte von } W^{(N)}
        }
    \right)^T
    \\
    &=
    \left(
        \bm{a}_1 \bm{p}_1 
        \cdots
        \bm{a}_1 \bm{p}_n
        \bigg|
        \bm{a}_2 \bm{p}_1 
        \cdots
        \bm{a}_2 \bm{p}_n
        \bigg|
        \cdots
        \bigg|
        \bm{a}_h \bm{p}_1
        \cdots
        \bm{a}_h \bm{p}_n
    \right)^T
\end{align*}

Für die einzelne Komponente gilt:
% (df/dW^N)*p
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{i=1}^{n} 
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}_{jk}}
    \cdot \bm{p}_i
    =
    \sum_{i=1}^{n} 
    \delta_{i\blau{j}}
    \cdot 
    \bm{a}_k \cdot \bm{p}_i
    =
    \bm{a}_k \cdot \bm{p}_j
\end{align*}

Für den Beitrag zum Gradienten gilt:
% J_WN
\begin{align*}
    \nabla_{W^{(N)}} J(\bm{u})
    =
    \bm{p} \cdot \bm{a}^T
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitung $\left(\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial b^{(1)}}\right)^T \bm{p}(t)$
% (df/db^1)*p
\begin{align*}
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial b^{(1)}_1} 
        &
        \cdots
        &
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial b^{(1)}_1}
        \\
        \vdots
        &
        \ddots
        &
        \vdots
        \\
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial b^{(1)}_h}
        &
        \cdots
        &
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial b^{(1)}_h}
    \end{pmatrix}
    \cdot
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \bm{p}_1 
        \\
        \vdots
        \\
        \bm{p}_n
    \end{pmatrix}
    =
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial b^{(1)}_1} \cdot \bm{p}_i
        \\
        \vdots
        \\
        \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial b^{(1)}_h} \cdot \bm{p}_i
    \end{pmatrix}
    =
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \bm{\alpha}_1 \bm{\delta}_1 
        \\
        \vdots
        \\
        \bm{\alpha}_h \bm{\delta}_h
    \end{pmatrix}
    =
    \bm{\alpha} \odot \bm{\delta}
\end{align*}

Für die einzelnen Komponenten gilt: 
%
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial b^{(1)}_j} \cdot \bm{p}_i
    =
    \sum_{i=1}^{n} W^{(N)}_{ij} \bm{\alpha}_j \cdot \bm{p}_i
    =
    \bm{\alpha}_j \bm{\delta}_j
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitung $\left(\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial b^{(N)}}\right)^T \bm{p}(t)$
% (df/db^N)*p
\begin{align*}
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial b^{(N)}_1} 
        &
        \cdots
        &
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial b^{(N)}_1}
        \\
        \vdots
        &
        \ddots
        &
        \vdots
        \\
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_1}{\partial b^{(N)}_n}
        &
        \cdots
        &
        \frac{\partial \bm{f}_n}{\partial b^{(N)}_n}
    \end{pmatrix}
    \cdot
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \bm{p}_1 
        \\
        \vdots
        \\
        \bm{p}_n
    \end{pmatrix}
    =
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial b^{(N)}_1} \cdot \bm{p}_i
        \\
        \vdots
        \\
        \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial b^{(N)}_n} \cdot \bm{p}_i
    \end{pmatrix}
    =
    \begin{pmatrix}
        \bm{p}_1 
        \\
        \vdots
        \\
        \bm{p}_n
    \end{pmatrix}
    =
    \bm{p}(t)
\end{align*}

Für die einzelnen Komponenten gilt: 
% df/db^N * p
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{\partial \bm{f}_i}{\partial b^{(N)}_j} \cdot \bm{p}_i
    =
    \sum_{i=1}^{n} \delta_{ij} \cdot \bm{p}_i
    =
    \bm{p}_j
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitung unserer Zielfunktion gilt:
% Berechnung J_u
\begin{align*}
    \nabla_{\bm{u}} J(\bm{u})
    &= 
    \int_{t_0}^{t_f} \left(\frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial \bm{u}}\right)^T \cdot \bm{p}(t) \dt
    =
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \int_{t_0}^{t_f} 
        \left(
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(1)}}
        \right)^T 
        \cdot 
        \bm{p}(t)
        \dt 
        \\[0.2cm]
        \int_{t_0}^{t_f} 
        \left(
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial b^{(1)}}
        \right)^T
        \cdot 
        \bm{p}(t) 
        \dt
        \\[0.2cm]
        \int_{t_0}^{t_f} 
        \left(
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial W^{(N)}}
        \right)^T
        \cdot 
        \bm{p}(t) 
        \dt
        \\[0.2cm]
        \int_{t_0}^{t_f} 
        \left(
            \frac{\partial \bm{f}}{\partial b^{(N)}}
        \right)^T
        \cdot 
        \bm{p}(t) 
        \dt
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

\subsection{LNN Herleitung}

Seien $M$ Trejaktorien zu $N$ Zeitpunkten im Zustandsraum $\mathbb{R}^2$ vorhanden, so speichern
wir die Information in $\bm{Q}, \bm{\dot{Q}}, \bm{U} , \bm{\ddot{Q}} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times M}$.
%
\begin{align*}
    \bm{Q}
    =
    \begin{bmatrix}
        q_{1}^{(0)} & \cdots & q_{1}^{(M-1)} \\
        \vdots      & \ddots & \vdots \\
        q_{N}^{(0)} & \cdots & q_{N}^{(M-1)}
    \end{bmatrix}
    \quad
    \bm{Q}_i 
    =
    \begin{bmatrix}
        q_{i}^{(0)} & \cdots & q_{i}^{(M-1)}
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

Wir definieren weiter $\bm{X} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times 2 \times M}$ 
und die Auswertung in einem Zeitpunkt als $\bm{X}_i \in \mathbb{R}^{2 \times M}$ durch
% Definition X = [Q \\ Q']
\begin{align*}
    \bm{X}_i = 
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \bm{Q}_i \\
        \bm{\dot{Q}}_i 
    \end{bmatrix}
    =
    \begin{bmatrix}
        q_{i}^{(0)}         & \cdots & q_{i}^{(M-1)} \\
        \dot{q}_{i}^{(0)}   & \cdots & \dot{q}_{i}^{(M-1)}
    \end{bmatrix}
    \quad
    \left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right]
    =
    \begin{bmatrix}
        q^{(j)}_i \\
        \dot{q}^{(j)}_i
    \end{bmatrix}
\end{align*}

Für die Lagrange-Funktion gilt nun mit Aktivierungsfunktion $\bm{\sigma}$
% L(X) also im Batch Format
\begin{align*}
    \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i)
    &=
    \underbrace{
        W^{(N)}
    }_{1 \times h}
    \bm{\sigma}
    \left(
        \underbrace{
            W^{(1)}
            \cdot
            \bm{X}_i
        }_{h \times M}
        +
        b^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T
    \right) + 
    \underbrace{
        b^{(N)} \bm{1}_M^T
    }_{1 \times M}
    \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times M}
\end{align*}

Wir definieren $\bm{A} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times h \times M}$ und ausgewertet in einem Zeitpunkt $t_i$ als 
$\bm{A}_i \in \mathbb{R}^{h \times M}$ durch: 
% Definition von A, alpha, und z
\begin{align*}
    \left[\bm{A}^{(j)}_i\right]_k
    &:= 
    \bm{\sigma}
    \left(
        \sum_{s=1}^{2} W_{ks}^{(1)} \left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right]_s  + b_k^{(1)}
    \right) 
    \quad
    \left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k
    := 
    \sum_{s=1}^{2} W_{ks}^{(1)} \left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right]_s  + b_k^{(1)}
    \\
    \bm{A}_i 
    &:=
    \begin{bmatrix}
        \overset{\vert}{\underset{\vert}{\bm{A}_i^{(0)}}}  
        &
        \cdots
        &
        \overset{\vert}{\underset{\vert}{\bm{A}_i^{(M-1)}}} 
    \end{bmatrix}
    =
    \bm{\sigma}
    \left[
        W^{(1)} \cdot \bm{X}_i + b^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T 
    \right] 
\end{align*}

Wir verwenden für die Aktivierungsfunktion $\tanh$ und $\mathsf{softplus}$:\footnote{
    Let $f,g$ be defined as $f(z):= \tanh(z)$ and $g(z) = \log\left(1 + e^z\right)$, the derivatives are given by
    %
    \begin{align*}
        % f'' = -2 f f' 
        f'(z) &= 1 - \tanh(z)^2  
        \quad 
        f''(z) 
        =
        (-2) \cdot f(z) \cdot f'(z)
        \quad 
        % f''' = -2f' f' + (-2) f (f'') =  -2f'^2 -2 f (-2f f') = f' (-2 (1-f^2) + 4f^2 )   = 6 f' f^2 - 2 f'
        f^{(3)}(z) = (-2) \cdot f'(z) \left(1-f(z)^2\right) +4 \cdot f(z)^2 f'(z) = 6f'(z) f(z)^2 - 2f'(z)
        \\
        g'(z) &= \frac{e^z}{1 + e^z} 
        \quad
        g''(z) = \frac{e^z}{\left(1 +e^{z}\right)^2}
        =
        g'(z) \cdot (1-g'(z))
        \quad
        % g'' = g' 1/(1+e^z)  1/(1+e^z)' = -1/(1+e^z)^2 *e^z = -g'
        % (g' 1/(1+e^z))' = g'' 1/(1+e^z) - g'g' 
        g^{(3)}(z) = g''(z) \cdot \left[1-2 \cdot g'(z)\right] 
    \end{align*}
}

% Übersicht Ableitungen softplus und tanh
\begin{center}
    \begin{tabular}{c | c | c}
        & $\tanh$ & $\mathsf{softplus}$ \\
        \hline
        % 1 Zeile mit A
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left[\bm{A}^{(j)}_i\right]_k := \bm{\sigma}\left(\left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k\right)
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \tanh\!\left(\left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k \right)
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \log\!\left(1 + \exp\left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k\right)
        \end{aligned}$ \\
        % 2 Zeile mit sigma' = alpha
        \hline
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left[\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}_i\right]_k :=\frac{\partial \bm{\sigma}(\bm{z})}{\partial \bm{z}} \bigg|_{\bm{z} = \left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            1 - \left[\bm{A}^{(j)}_i\right]_k^2
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \frac{\exp\left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k}{1 + \exp\left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k}
        \end{aligned}$ 
        \\
        % 3 Zeile mit sigma'' = beta
        \hline
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left[\bm{\beta}^{(j)}_i\right]_k :=\frac{\partial \bm{\alpha}(\bm{z})}{\partial \bm{z}} \bigg|_{\bm{z} = \left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            (-2) \cdot \left[\bm{A}^{(j)}_i\right]_k \cdot  \left[\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}_i\right]_k
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left[\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}_i\right]_k \cdot \left(1 - \left[\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}_i\right]_k \right)
        \end{aligned}$ 
        \\
        % 4 Zeile mit sigma''' = gamma
        \hline
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left[\bm{\gamma}^{(j)}_i\right]_k :=\frac{\partial \bm{\beta}(\bm{z})}{\partial \bm{z}} \bigg|_{\bm{z} = \left[\bm{z}_i^{(j)}\right]_k}
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            2 \cdot  \left[\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}_i\right]_k \cdot \left( 3 \cdot \left[\bm{A}^{(j)}_i\right]_k^2 -1 \right)
        \end{aligned}$
        &
        $\begin{aligned}
            \left[\bm{\beta}^{(j)}_i\right]_k \cdot \left(1 - 2 \cdot  \left[\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}_i\right]_k \right) 
        \end{aligned}$ 
    \end{tabular}
\end{center}

Wir definieren $\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta}, \bm{\gamma} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times h \times M}$ und für einen festen Zeitpunkt
als $\bm{\alpha}_i,\bm{\beta}_i, \bm{\gamma}_i \in \mathbb{R}^{h \times M}$. 

Sei $h$ gegeben dann gilt mit $\bm{\theta} \in \mathbb{R}^{4h +1}$ 
für die ersten Ableitungen der Lagrange-Funktion:
\footnote{
    For a fixed state $\left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right] = \begin{bmatrix} q_i^{(j)} & \dot{q}_i^{(j)} \end{bmatrix}^T$ 
    and time the Lagrangian is a scalar
    %
    \begin{align*}
        \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i^{(j)})
        &=
        \sum_{k=1}^{h}
        W^{(N)}_{1k} 
        \cdot 
        \sigma
        \left(
            \sum_{s=1}^{2} W^{(1)}_{ks} \left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right]_s +  b^{(1)}_k
        \right)
        + b^{(N)}
    \end{align*}

    hence the partial derivatives with respect to $q$ and $\dot{q}$ are given by 
    %
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i^{(j)})}{\partial q}
        &=
        \sum_{k=1}^{h}
        W^{(N)}_{1k} 
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial}{\partial z} \sigma\left(z = \sum_{s=1}^{2} W^{(1)}_{ks} \left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right]_s +  b^{(1)}_k \right)
        \cdot 
        W^{(1)}_{k1} 
        =
        W^{(N)} \cdot 
        \left(
            \left[\bm{\alpha}_i^{(j)}\right] \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}
        \right)
        \\
        \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i^{(j)})}{\partial \dot{q}}
        &=
        \sum_{k=1}^{h}
        W^{(N)}_{1k}
        \cdot 
        \frac{\partial}{\partial z} \sigma\left(z = \sum_{s=1}^{2} W^{(1)}_{ks} \left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right]_s +  b^{(1)}_k \right)
        \cdot 
        W^{(1)}_{k2}
        =
        W^{(N)} \cdot 
        \left(
            \left[\bm{\alpha}_i^{(j)}\right] \odot \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
        \right)
    \end{align*}
    %
    where $\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(s)}$ denotes the $s$-th column of the Matrix $W^{(1)}$. 
}
% Berechnung dL/dq und dL/dqdot
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i)}{\partial q}
    &=
    W^{(N)} \cdot 
    \left[
        \bm{\alpha}_i
        \odot 
        \left(    
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}
            \cdot 
            \bm{1}_M^T
        \right)
    \right]
    \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times M}
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i)}{\partial \dot{q}}
    &=
    W^{(N)} \cdot 
    \left(
        \bm{\alpha}_i 
        \odot 
        \left(    
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
            \cdot 
            \bm{1}_M^T
        \right)
    \right)
    \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times M}
\end{align*}


Für die Ableitung von $\partial_{q} \bm{L}$ koponentweise nach den Parametern gilt: 
% d/dtheta (dLdq) berechnen:
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial}{\partial W_{k1}^{(1)}} \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i^{(j)})}{\partial q}
    &=
    % Hier Ableitung nach W_{j1}^(1) komponentenweise:
    W^{(N)}_{1k} \cdot 
    \left(
        \left[\bm{\beta}_i^{(j)}\right]_k
        \cdot
        \left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right]_1 
        \cdot 
        W^{(1)}_{k1} 
        +
        \left[\bm{\alpha}_i^{(j)}\right]_k
    \right)
    \\
    \frac{\partial}{\partial W_{k2}^{(1)}} \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i^{(j)})}{\partial q}
    &=
    % Hier Ableitung nach W_{j2}^{(1)} komponentenweise:
    W^{(N)}_{1k} \cdot \left[\bm{\beta}_i^{(j)}\right]_k \cdot \left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)} \right]_2 \cdot  W^{(1)}_{k1}
    \\
    \frac{\partial}{\partial b_{k}^{(1)}} \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i^{(j)})}{\partial q}
    &=
    % Hier Ableitung nach b_{k}^{(1)}:
    W^{(N)}_{1k} \cdot \left[\bm{\beta}_i^{(j)}\right]_k \cdot W^{(1)}_{k1}
    \\
    \frac{\partial}{\partial W_{1k}^{(N)}} \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i^{(j)})}{\partial q}
    &=
    % Hier Ableitung nach W_{1k}^{(N)}:
    \left[\bm{\alpha}_i^{(j)}\right]_k \cdot W_{k1}^{(1)}
\end{align*}

Für alle Trajektorien parallel gilt im Batch-Format:
% Batch Format:
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial}{\partial \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}} \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i)}{\partial q}
    &= 
    % Ableitung nach [W^{(1)}]^(1) Batch Format:
    \left[\left(W^{(N)} \right)^T \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right] 
    \odot 
    \left(
        \bm{\beta}_i 
        \odot
        \left[\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{Q}_i\right]
        \odot
        \left[
            \left(W^{(1)}\right)^{(1)}
            \cdot 
            \bm{1}_M^T
        \right]
        +
        \bm{\alpha}_i
    \right)
    \\
    \frac{\partial}{\partial \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}} \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i)}{\partial q}
    &=
    % Hier Ableitung nach [W^{(1)}]^(2) Batch Format:
    \left[\left(W^{(N)} \right)^T \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right]
    \odot 
    \bm{\beta}_i 
    \odot
    \left[\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{\dot{Q}}_i\right]
    \odot
    \left[
        \left(W^{(1)}\right)^{(1)}
        \cdot 
        \bm{1}_M^T
    \right]
    \\
    \frac{\partial}{\partial b^{(1)}} \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i)}{\partial q}
    &=
    % Hier Ableitung nach b^{(1)} im Batch Format:
    \left[\left(W^{(N)} \right)^T \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right]
    \odot
    \bm{\beta}_i 
    \odot
    \left[
        \left(W^{(1)}\right)^{(1)}
        \cdot 
        \bm{1}_M^T
    \right]
    \\
    \frac{\partial}{\partial W^{(N)}} \frac{\partial \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i)}{\partial q}
    &=
    \bm{\alpha}_i 
    \odot 
    \left[
        \left(W^{(1)}\right)^{(1)}
        \cdot 
        \bm{1}_M^T
    \right]
\end{align*}

Für die zweiten Ableitungen der Lagrange-Funktion gilt:
\footnote{
For a fixed state $\left[\bm{X}_i^{(j)}\right] = \begin{bmatrix} q_i^{(j)} & \dot{q}_i^{(j)} \end{bmatrix}^T$ 
the second order partial derivatives are given by
%
\begin{align*}
    C_{i}^{(j)}
    &=
    \frac{\partial^2 \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i^{(j)})}{\partial \dot{q} \partial q}
    =
    \sum_{k=1}^{h}
    W^{(N)}_{1k} \cdot
    \left[\bm{\beta}_i^{(j)}\right]_k
    \cdot 
    W^{(1)}_{k1}
    \cdot 
    W^{(1)}_{k2} 
    = 
    W^{(N)} 
    \cdot 
    \left(
        \bm{\beta}_i^{(j)}
        \odot
        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}
        \odot 
        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
    \right)
    \in \mathbb{R}
    \\
    H_{i}^{(j)}
    &=
    \frac{\partial^2 \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i^{(j)})}{\partial \dot{q}^2}
    =
    \sum_{k=1}^{h} W_{1k}^{(N)} 
    \cdot 
    \left[\bm{\beta}_i^{(j)}\right]_k
    \cdot 
    \left[W^{(1)}_{k2}\right]^2
    = 
    W^{(N)} \cdot 
    \left(
        \bm{\beta}_i^{(j)}
        \odot
        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
        \odot 
        \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
    \right)
    \in \mathbb{R}
\end{align*}
}

% Berechnung C und H
\begin{align*}
    \bm{C}_{i}
    &=
    \frac{\partial^2 \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i)}{\partial \dot{q} \partial q}
    =
    W^{(N)}
    \cdot 
    \left[
        \bm{\beta}_i
        \odot
        \left(        
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T
        \right)
        \odot 
        \left(
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T
        \right)
    \right]
    \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times M}
    \\
    \bm{H}_{i}
    &=
    \frac{\partial^2 \bm{L}(\bm{X}_i)}{\partial \dot{q}^2}
    =
    W^{(N)}
    \cdot 
    \left[
        \bm{\beta}_i
        \odot
        \left(        
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}
            \cdot
            \bm{1}_M^T
        \right)
        \odot 
        \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right)
    \right]
    \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times M}
\end{align*}


Für die Ableitungen von $\bm{H}_i$ nach den Parametern gilt: 
% dH/dtheta
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{H}_i}{\partial \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}}
    &=
    \left(
        \bm{1}_M \cdot W^{(N)} 
    \right)^T
    \odot
    \left[
        \bm{\gamma}_i
        \odot
        \left(\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{Q}_i\right)
        \odot
        \left(        
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T
        \right)
        \odot 
        \left(
            \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T
        \right)
    \right]
    \in \mathbb{R}^{h \times M}
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{H}_i}{\partial \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}}
    &=
    \left(
        \bm{1}_M \cdot W^{(N)}
    \right)^T
    \odot 
    \left(\left[W^{(1)} \right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \left[
        \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right)
        \odot
        \bm{\gamma}_i 
        \odot
        \left(
            \bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{\dot{Q}}_i
        \right)
        + 2\bm{\beta}_i
    \right]
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{H}_i}{\partial b^{(1)}}
    &=
    \left(\bm{1}_M \cdot W^{(N)}\right)^T
    \odot
    \bm{\gamma}_i 
    \odot 
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right)
    \odot
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right)
    \\
    \frac{\partial }{\partial W^{(N)}}
    &=
    \bm{\beta}_i
    \odot
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right)
    \odot
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right)
\end{align*}

Für die Ableitungen von $\bm{C}_i$ nach den Parametern gilt: 
% dC/dtheta
\begin{align*} 
    \frac{\partial \bm{C}_i}{\partial \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}}
    &=
    \left(\bm{1}_M \cdot W^{(N)}\right)^T
    \odot 
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \left[
        \bm{\gamma}_i 
        \odot 
        (\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{Q}_i) 
        \odot 
        \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
        + \bm{\beta}_i
    \right] 
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{C}_i}{\partial \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}}
    &=
    \left(\bm{1}_M \cdot W^{(N)}\right)^T
    \odot 
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \left[
        \bm{\gamma}_i 
        \odot 
        (\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{\dot{Q}}_i) 
        \odot 
        \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
        + \bm{\beta}_i
    \right] 
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{C}_i}{\partial b^{(1)}}
    &=
    \left(\bm{1}_M \cdot W^{(N)}\right)^T
    \odot 
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \bm{\gamma}_i
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{C}_i}{\partial W^{(N)}}
    &=
    \bm{\beta}_i
    \odot
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
\end{align*}

Für 
$\bm{N}_i := \frac{\partial \bm{L}_i}{\partial q} 
- \bm{C}_i \cdot \dot{q} + u$ 
erhalten wir: 
$\frac{\partial \bm{N}_i}{\partial \bm{\theta}} 
= 
\frac{\partial}{\partial \bm{\theta}} 
\frac{\partial \bm{L}_i}{\partial q}
- \frac{\partial \bm{C}_i}{\partial \bm{\theta}} \dot{q} 
$
% dN/dtheta N=L_qdot - C qdot + u
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{N}_i}{\partial \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)}}
    &=
    % Hier Ableitung nach W^1^{(1)}:
    \left[\left(W^{(N)} \right)^T \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right] 
    \odot 
    \left(
        \bm{\beta}_i 
        \odot
        \left[\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{Q}_i\right]
        \odot
        \left[
            \left(W^{(1)}\right)^{(1)}
            \cdot 
            \bm{1}_M^T
        \right]
        +
        \bm{\alpha}_i
    \right)
    \\
    &\quad -
    \left[\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{\dot{Q}}_i \right] \odot
    % Hier C nach W^1^{(1)}:
    \left(\bm{1}_M \cdot W^{(N)}\right)^T
    \odot 
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \left[
        \bm{\gamma}_i 
        \odot 
        (\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{Q}_i) 
        \odot 
        \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
        + \bm{\beta}_i
    \right]
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{N}_i}{\partial \left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)}}
    &=
    % Ableitung nach W^1^{(2)}:
    \left[\left(W^{(N)} \right)^T \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right]
    \odot 
    \bm{\beta}_i 
    \odot
    \left[\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{\dot{Q}}_i\right]
    \odot
    \left[
        \left(W^{(1)}\right)^{(1)}
        \cdot 
        \bm{1}_M^T
    \right]
    \\
    &\quad -
    \left[\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{\dot{Q}}_i \right] \odot
    % Hier C nach W^1^{(2)}:
    \left(\bm{1}_M \cdot W^{(N)}\right)^T
    \odot 
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \left[
        \bm{\gamma}_i 
        \odot 
        (\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{\dot{Q}}_i) 
        \odot 
        \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
        + \bm{\beta}_i
    \right]
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{N}_i}{\partial b_{j}^{(1)}}
    &=
    % Ableitung nach b^{(1)}:
    \left[\left(W^{(N)} \right)^T \cdot \bm{1}_M^T\right]
    \odot
    \bm{\beta}_i 
    \odot
    \left[
        \left(W^{(1)}\right)^{(1)}
        \cdot 
        \bm{1}_M^T
    \right]
    \\
    &\quad -
    \left[\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{\dot{Q}}_i \right] \odot
    \left(\bm{1}_M \cdot W^{(N)}\right)^T
    \odot 
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \bm{\gamma}_i
    \\
    \frac{\partial \bm{N}_i}{\partial W_{1j}^{(N)}}
    &=
    % Ableitung nach W^{(N)}:
    \bm{\alpha}_i 
    \odot 
    \left[
        \left(W^{(1)}\right)^{(1)}
        \cdot 
        \bm{1}_M^T
    \right]
    \\
    &\quad -
    \left[\bm{1}_h \cdot \bm{\dot{Q}}_i \right] \odot
    % Hier C nach W^{(N)}:
    \bm{\beta}_i
    \odot
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(1)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
    \odot
    \left(\left[W^{(1)}\right]^{(2)} \cdot \bm{1}_M^T \right)
\end{align*}

Wir erhalten für den Gradienten: 
% dJ/dtheta
\begin{align*}
    \nabla_{\bm{\theta}} J(\bm{\theta}) 
    &=
    \frac{1}{N \cdot M}
    \sum_{j=0}^{M-1} \sum_{i=0}^{N-1} \bm{p}_i^{(j)}
    \cdot
    \left[
        - \frac{\partial H_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
        \bm{\ddot{q}_{\theta}}
        +
        \frac{\partial N_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}}
    \right]
    \quad
    % Definition des Adjungierten Zustands in dem Setting:
    \dunkelrot{
        \bm{p}_i^{(j)} := 2H^{-1}_{\bm{\theta}} 
        \left[
            \ddot{q}
            \left(
                q_i^{(j)}, \dot{q}_i^{(j)}, u_i^{(j)}
            \right) 
            - 
            \ddot{q}_{i}^{(j)}
        \right]
    }
\end{align*}

\subsection{Exponentialansatz im strukturierten Fall}

\begin{align*}
    \frac{\partial \bm{f}_2}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    &=
    -\frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}^2} \cdot 
    \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \cdot 
    \left(
        -\frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}' \bm{x}_2^2 - V_{\bm{\theta}}' + u
    \right)
    +
    \frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}}
    \cdot 
    \left(
        -\frac{\bm{x}_2^2}{2} \frac{\partial M_{\bm{\theta}}'}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \right)
    \\
    &=
    -\frac{1}{M_{\bm{\theta}}} \cdot 
    \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \cdot 
    \left(
        -\frac{1}{2} M_{\bm{\theta}}' \bm{x}_2^2 - V_{\bm{\theta}}' + u
    \right)
    - \frac{\bm{x}_2^2}{2}
    \cdot
    \left(
        \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
        \cdot
        z_{\bm{\theta}}'
        +
        \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}'}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \right)
    \\
    &=
    - \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z} \cdot \bm{f}_2
    - \frac{\bm{x}_2^2}{2}
    \cdot
    \left(
        \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
        \cdot
        z_{\bm{\theta}}'
        +
        \frac{\partial z_{\bm{\theta}}'}{\partial \bm{\theta}_z}
    \right)
\end{align*}
